## What is on the page at Chapter 360, and what this batch did to it

**The ten weeks moved nothing that the volume's rule forbids moving, and they moved the prose of the batch itself, which is the only thing in this record that moved at all.** A practice in two people about four hundred and thirty miles apart continues with no list, no form, no heading and no fund. A woman of twenty in a counting room is still not paid and is still not going to ask what it is held against, and the sixteenth book was not asked for. A clerk of thirty-four at the top of a stair in Auremar has still said the whole of a rule out loud four times and has not been asked one thing back, and the box of about four hundred blanks is still under the counter and is still not a post. A woman of twenty-four at the other end of the same counter has still never been asked anything, and her undertaking is unretired, unamended and unexercised, and a correct non-use is entered as one. A man of thirty-eight has refused the reading and has not exercised the thing he is able to, and the reason he is not at a stage is not said in this volume. A foreman of fifty-one is still inside a box entered by a class and nobody has asked her about it, and the two of seventeen at the back of that bay are still unspoken to, unthanked, unsent for and not going to start, and the four who cannot make sense of a paragraph were not asked one question about a number, and the man of about fifty-five with a chain is still not one of them. The man of about sixty-one is still the second hand, still cannot read a paragraph, still signs about nine hundred sheets a year, and about nine of them a year are still not what the person above him wrote and he signed them all. The woman of about twenty-six at the end of the cold passage has not handed her figure on and has asked none of her three questions. The man of about thirty-four has a number in his head and has not given it away, and the bill for being findable is still not his and still stands on somebody else's account.

**The count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is seven, it was seven at Chapter 350, and it is seven at Chapter 360. The next asking is nobody's yet and this batch did not make it anybody's. Nobody was thanked, nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for, and nothing was resolved. The two people in their boxes stay in them and the form that put them there is not asked to be void. The question on the clerk of about fifty-five's shelf is still a question, carries no full stop, and has her own name at the foot of it, and a quarter of it was answered out loud in a room at the far end of her own passage in the second month by a man who walked nine miles to get there, and she wrote none of that down and is not going to be asked about it again. The figure at the end of the cold passage is not handed on and does not get a heading. The guarantee is not printed in its own words and no child is named in it, and about nineteen people are still behind the bank. The bill at Lowcross stands at nineteen pounds three and fourpence, unpaid, nobody liable, and this batch named it zero times and printed no new money total and invented no wage. The four hundred and thirty miles was walked zero times and the nine miles was walked zero times. The quarterly return does not appear and is in no reply; the other return of the eleventh of the tenth is dormant and armed and known to nobody in the story; the four appearances of the substituted wording keep their four ages; the three stitches stay three things; the two sealed pages stay shut with two refusals between them and no third, and the sealed witness house was not visited; the counting-house mark is a mark and no chapter converted it; forty-four thousand eight hundred and nineteen is not printed. The Venn ruling has five operative terms, was never said to have four, and its schedule is referred to zero times. The Lowcross bill, the guarantee, the two documents, the quarterly return, the counting-house mark, the Venn schedule, the hiring store's two names, the engagement's two datings and the Chapter 0145 dating phrase are all carried unrepaired.**

# THE REPAIR OF VOLUME 08 BATCH 0002 — THE FACTS IT SETTLED, THE FACT IT CORRECTED, AND WHAT IT DID NOT MOVE

*Appended downward by the phase that read `logs/batch-0002.review.log`. **A repair is not a review, it reviewed nothing, and it discharges none of the four debts. Nothing above this point is rewritten and no status in any section above it is changed.** Three facts about the page are settled here that the review found unsettled, one is corrected, and the locked facts are restated so that a later writer has them in one place after the repair.*

**The man of about fifty-five with the chain.** The page now says one thing about him and says it once. **The one thing he is able to do reached him about four years ago, at a shut gate at the end of a lane with a man at it and nothing else in the world, and it has not reached him since, and he has not so much as turned it over to look at it, and nobody at the front of that bay knows that he knows it.** The first draft of Chapter 0364 said both that he had known for about four years that it would not reach him and that he had used it about four years ago and it worked, which is the same four years twice, and the repair reconciles it the only way the closed page allows: it worked once and it has not worked since, and his knowledge dates from that gate. He is still not one of the four men in that bay who cannot make sense of a paragraph and still says so out loud to nobody, and he is still not asked one question about a number, and the chain is still not a symbol. **The non-use is now staged and not only asserted: twice in one afternoon his hand goes to the bench beside the slate and twice the bay goes on being a bay.** He does not use it, the slate is on the bench face up at the end of the chapter, and the volume's power-system noun is not used and the reason is not restated, which is Chapter 0305's payment and is not paid twice.

**The bay's population.** The man of about twenty-eight no longer claims that two people out of about nine hundred cannot read what is on a slate in that bay, which contradicted closed Chapter 0356's line that four men in that bay cannot make sense of a paragraph. **He now says that he and the man with the chain cannot read a slate, that there are four men behind them who cannot make a paragraph out of anything, and that it is a different complaint, and that he has never worked out which of the two of them would come off worse.** The four men in 0356 stand, and the narrow reading survives, and no chapter in this batch now makes a claim about how many people in this empire cannot do a thing that the closed page contradicts.

**The four witnesses in Chapter 0365 are four people.** A man with a tray who had to come up those four steps to get past the two of them and stood in the doorway with it in both hands until they were clear, and a woman at the far end of that floor who came in for a stamp and went straight back out without stopping and did not look at the box. **The chapter's contribution is now shown and not reported, and the count of about four is a count of bodies in a doorway and not a figure anybody could enter.** No form records any of it, none of the four was thanked, none was asked for anything, and the box is on the floor of the received room with the lid where it was and is still under that counter as far as any form is concerned.

**The frames.** Every one of the ten chapters carries the `no form` frame under a stated reading, and the ten primary two-word openings are all fresh against Chapters 001 to 360 on the repaired page: *occupying a*, *hearing what*, *lodging a*, *trying a*, *witnessing a*, *saying a rule*, *shutting that*, *disagreeing with*, *speaking about*, *the serving*. **Chapters 0362, 0366, 0367, 0368 and 0369 carry a wording the first draft did not have, and the two that had the same idea in three different chapters — the form for asking somebody whether they would mind being told a thing first — are in 0361 and 0365 and are not in 0369, which carries the two women and the man four feet apart instead.** The standing phrase *there is no form* is still used zero times in the ten files and the frame is still never printed as a count in a chapter.

**What did not move, restated after the repair.** A copy is fourpence and a question is a shilling and a certificate is four shillings, and a carrier is paid ninepence at a lock, and none of them moved. A reader is paid forty-five pence a day and four days a week. A foreman's job on that cut came to Halla Wray because nine families agreed it in a shed, and the class she is in is still a heading. **The bar of Marek Kest's own came off him on the twelfth of the ninth month of last year on an application of one line which he wrote himself, and the mark about three inches long on the inside of his left wrist reads nothing and decides nothing, and the repair touched neither.** No new money total, no new wage figure, no new named figure, and no figure on the page altered. **The three people the state layer keeps apart stayed apart: the man of about sixty-one is in 0368, the woman of about twenty-six is in none of the ten, and the woman of twenty is in 0366, in three chapters with three rooms, and no chapter states or denies a distance between any two of them, and the woman of about forty with a scuttle in 0362 is a fourth person and is not the woman of about twenty-six.** The two documents stay two documents with no instrument joining them. The quarterly return does not appear. The other return of the eleventh of the tenth is dormant and armed and known to nobody in the story. The four appearances of the substituted wording keep their four ages and this batch adds no fifth. The three stitches stay three things. The two sealed pages stay shut and the sealed witness house is not visited. Forty-four thousand eight hundred and nineteen is not printed. The counting-house mark is a mark and the chalk mark in the bay is a mark and no chapter converted either. **The bill at Lowcross is still nineteen pounds three and fourpence and was named zero times in these ten files, which is the third batch in a row. The four hundred and thirty miles was walked zero times and the nine miles was walked zero times. The count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is seven at Chapter 350, at 360 and at 370, and the next asking is nobody's yet.**

**And the four debts, restated in full at the end of this repair, which discharges none of them.** An owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, four volumes on. Owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's own Batch 0003, which is four and not three. The review gate, which opened and produced findings on all seven Batch 0005 phases the commit history records, and has fallen back to the writing agent every time it has been asked, and has never once produced a review that could be certified independent, and a gate that only ever falls back is not a gate that passed either. And the open items that are not plot, with the sentence bound and the unit columns among them. **The number of debts is four and the number of phases that have claimed to discharge one is zero, and this record is not among them.**

# THE CONTINUITY RECORD OF VOLUME 08 BATCH 0003, CHAPTERS 0371 TO 0380, THE SEASON TURNS AND THE SAME ARRANGEMENTS COST MORE

*Appended downward by the run that took `workspace/volume-08/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`. **Nothing above this point is rewritten. This is a writing phase's record and not a review, it reviewed nothing, and it discharges none of the four debts.** The measurement blocks of this record are the batch's own, measured after the last edit to any chapter, with the method before each figure, and every figure that does not reproduce is named as not reproducing rather than corrected into a band.*

## The ten weeks, in one paragraph, and the story of them

**Two chapters in the sixth month, four in the seventh and four in the eighth, one chapter to a week, and the day inside the week checks out against the inherited cycle in all ten rows.** The season turned and nothing was altered. A man of thirty-eight who chose a labour he was free to turn down was not stood at the bar at the end of a yard for the first time in about two years, because the cold meant nobody had to be stood there, and the man who set the rate said both figures out loud and was not the one who was missing. A stove in a counting room four hundred and thirty miles up this river was out at the second hour and the second of four women came to be given a rule a third time and was refused, and went down the lane with a twist of her own kindling in the firebox. All four rooms of a lodging house were let for the first time in a while and a woman of twenty-two said a thing out loud in the building that had been one woman's alone for two years, and it cannot be unsaid. A counter at the top of four flights shut at the sixth hour in weather, and a clerk of twenty-four went down all four flights to tell a person at the bottom that it was shut, which is telling and not asking. The written engagement of two seventeen-year-olds came to the end of its own terms on paper and nobody spoke to either of them and the girl was not given the chance to say no. A clerk of fifty-five entered four sheets before the fourth hour because the light is going, and a woman of nineteen who has been in the job eight weeks told her that her name is the only name at the foot of anything in that building. A man of sixty-one did not walk his nine miles, and heard third-hand that a man of thirty-eight once had, and that nobody at the far end was told to expect him. A man of thirty-four came down a lane at the sixth hour with a bill that has his name on it and is on somebody else's account, and neither woman said the thing that would make it an understanding. A man of thirty-eight paid fourpence for a copy at the top of four flights and the name he is after is not on it. And at the end of about four hundred yards of cold flags a count of persons under nothing went up again, and was not handed on, and got no heading.**

## The locks, each one named and each one held, and the way it was held

**Nobody asked anybody anything out loud, in ten chapters, in any form.** A question mark is punctuation and not an asking and the two counts are separate: there are two question marks across the ten files, one in Chapter 0373 and one in Chapter 0375, both of them in a bare short line in a mouth and both of them about an object — a fifth line in a day-book, said to the counter, and a rate at the foot of a paper, said to the paper. The count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is **seven**, it is seven at Chapter 350, 360 and 370, and it is seven at Chapter 380. **Nobody was thanked: every instance of the word in the ten files is a negation, and there are six of them.** Nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for, and nothing was resolved.

**The two people in their boxes stay in them and the form that put them there is not asked to be void by anybody.** Halla Wray is in the box entered by a class in Chapter 0375 and the man of about thirty-eight is in nothing at all in Chapter 0371 and pays fourpence for a copy in Chapter 0379. The question with no full stop stays a question, carries no period, its author is not asked a second time, and the woman of nineteen has not thrown it out. The figure at the end of the cold passage is not handed on, does not get a heading, and is not printed in any form. The guarantee is not printed in its own words and no child is named in it, and about nineteen people are still behind the bank. **The bill at Lowcross stands at nineteen pounds three and fourpence, unpaid, nobody liable, and this batch named it zero times, which is the third batch in a row and is entered as a decision and not as an oversight; the bill that Chapter 0378 puts in a hall is a different bill, carries no figure, and is on somebody else's account, and the page does not say the two are the same debt.** The four who cannot make sense of a paragraph are not asked one question about a number, and the man of about fifty-five with the chain and the man of about sixty-one are neither of them one of them and never are. A girl of seventeen and a reader of seventeen are unspoken to, unthanked, unsent for and not going to start, and the girl is not given the chance to say no. **The four hundred and thirty miles is walked zero times and the nine miles is walked zero times in the ten files.** The two documents stay two documents with no instrument joining them and only Marek Kest and Tamsin Rook know both exist and neither of them says so. The quarterly return does not appear. The other return of the eleventh of the tenth is dormant and armed and known to nobody in the story. The four appearances of the substituted wording keep their four ages and this batch adds no fifth. The three stitches stay three things. The two sealed pages stay shut with two refusals between them and no third, and the sealed witness house is not visited. The counting-house mark is a mark and no chapter converts it. Forty-four thousand eight hundred and nineteen is not printed. **The Venn ruling has five operative terms and is never said to have four, and its schedule is referred to zero times and the word does not occur in any of the ten files.**

**The three people the state layer keeps apart stayed apart.** The man of about sixty-one is in Chapter 0377 at a door nine miles off his work and is referred to in Chapter 0380 as a man at the other end of about four hundred yards of cold flags, and he is not in that passage. The woman of about twenty-six is in Chapter 0380 at the lock end of a passage off the back of that building and has never been in the room at the far end of it. The woman of twenty is in Chapter 0372 in a counting room about four hundred and thirty miles up this river, and is referred to in Chapter 0377 as a woman of twenty in a counting room with a wage in a coat four miles off. **The woman of about forty with a scuttle is in none of these ten files, which is entered and is not a repair, and no chapter puts any two of the four in one room.** The clerk of about fifty-five and the man of about sixty-one are in the same building in different rooms and no chapter puts them in one room this batch.

## The figures, and none of them altered

**The canon figures did not move.** The thirty-one, the eleven, the twelve and the six stand as they stood. About nine hundred and forty people and a guild's hundred and thirty-five shillings are two more and none of the five is a member of a pattern and no chapter puts two of them in one sentence as one. **The figures this batch turns on, and none of them is altered: three shillings for a day on a wall that is not on a district's list and a shilling for a day on a wall that is, and the man who decided between them is not at the bar; about nine hundred sheets a year; a second hand since he was forty-two; nine miles; forty-two; about four hundred blanks; eleven years; about two years; about four hundred yards; about four hundred and thirty miles; about nineteen days; about four thousand entries; about nine foot by eleven; four hundred people a season.** **The wages did not move and this batch printed no new money total and invented no new wage figure:** forty-five pence a day and four days a week for a reader; three pounds and eleven and threepence for nineteen days out of seventeen shillings and a penny the week, called *held*; seven shillings and sixpence is two days of that crew; three shillings and a shilling at a bar of iron; four shillings a year is the Company's whole money; fourpence a copy, a shilling a question, four shillings for a certificate, about eleven weeks for an answer, about four hundred forms in a box under a counter, ninepence for a carrier at a lock. **Item 26 held: no chapter dated a coming, counted a coming, confirmed the man of about sixty-one's figure or added to it.** The one instance of the figure is the man of about sixty-one himself keeping it, in Chapter 0377, and it is neither confirmed nor denied.

**And the two places where a plan and the page could have disagreed, and the page won, and the correction says which way it went.** *One:* the card for Chapter 0376 put two rooms in it, a ground-floor room off the old river road and a ground-floor room at the other end of about four hundred yards of cold flags, and the page has one room and a passage at the back of it and refers to the room with the rail from four hundred yards off without entering it, because the closed page places the clerk's room and the rail room in the same building with the passage between them. *Two:* the card for Chapter 0379 gave the paying of the fourpence to a man of about thirty-four with the fair hand and in the same sentence said that man was not in the chapter; the page has Marek Kest pay the fourpence, because the thing carried about two years is his page with a struck line in it and the man of thirty-four is not on that stair this week. **The page wins both, and the card file is not rewritten to match the page.**

## The rooms, referred to and not described again, and the twenty-word paragraph sweep is what held

Ten chapters, ten rooms, and four of them are rooms this project has already described in full: the end of a yard and its bar of iron, the counting room at the back of a dye end, the lodging house with its counter and shelf, the ground-floor room off the old river road. **None of the four is described again.** The shed at the Slade end is the one room this batch enters that the batches before it called a bay, and the page uses one name for that building and not two. The counter at the top of four flights is in 0374 and 0379 and the second refers to the first. **The twenty-word paragraph sweep against the closed page, run on each chapter as it was finished and not on the batch at the end, returned 12 and all twelve are required verbatim recurrences: ten dated weeks, the wage-held canon date in 0372, and the required closing count recurrence. The first draft of this batch returned accidents at this sweep in 0371, 0372, 0373, 0375, 0377, 0378, 0379 and 0380 and every one of them was rewritten.**

## The four debts, restated in full at Chapter 380, and this phase discharges none of them

**One: an owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, four volumes on. Two, three and four: owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's own Batch 0003, which is four and not three. Plus the review gate, which opened and produced findings on all seven Batch 0005 phases the commit history records, and has fallen back to the writing agent every time it has been asked, and has never once produced a review that could be certified independent, and a gate that only ever falls back is not a gate that passed either. And the open items that are not plot, with the sentence bound and the unit columns among them.** The number of debts has not moved in eight volumes and the number of phases that have claimed to discharge one is zero. The threads this batch did not touch are the who, the second half of the question, the two documents, the sealed pages, the bundle, the substituted wording and its four ages, the quarterly return, the other return of the eleventh of the tenth, the counting-house mark, the ninety-second leaf, the Venn ruling's schedule, the hiring store's two names, the engagement's two datings, the Chapter 0145 dating phrase, the four-page item, the guarantee's own words, and the sentence bound as a whole. **`state/phase-ledger.json` is not edited by this phase, is not this phase's to edit, and its reading is carried as item 43 and is not repeated here as current.**

## The independent reading of this batch, and the four things it found on the page, and the eight it did not fix

*An independent reading is not a review, it is not the review gate, and it discharges none of the four debts. It was produced by a separate hand given the ten files, fourteen context chapters and the outline of record, and it found three things in Chapter 0379 that the question-mark count could not see: an interrogative addressed across a counter, a question framed as addressed to a window and then answered by a person standing at the counter, and two further askings described as having happened. All three are gone and the chapter's own sentence that nobody asked him anything is now true, where for twenty-four lines it was not. It found Chapter 0380 re-running four consecutive lines of closed Chapter 0358's central dialogue, two of them verbatim, with no contiguous run longer than thirteen words. It found a fifteen-word clause lifted out of closed Chapter 0355 in Chapter 0371, **and it found that this record's own gloss of that file's fifteen-word figures as fragments of a required dated week was false for one of the two, which is a corrected figure and is corrected in `state/batch-summary.md` beside the wrong one.** It found Chapter 0379 printing the same sentence twice in consecutive paragraphs, and Chapter 0375's second speech turns unattributed. All four are fixed on the page. It did not fix, and this phase carried: the register, which is still one voice; the dated week, which is in the last section of seven of ten rather than varied; the six chapters that re-run a beat of a chapter in the batch before, and the two of those that are structural rather than verbal, one of which is repaired and one of which is carried as a debt because fixing it means rewriting a chapter; and Chapter 0376's relation to Chapter 0370, which is a different exchange in the same room with the same two women six chapters apart, now without a shared twenty-two-word closing run and now entered as a structural debt rather than left unrecorded.** The full findings and the two best passages are in `state/batch-summary.md` under the heading of the independent reading, and the check that could not see any of this is item 59, which is the third way a serial novel re-uses itself and is new in this ledger.**

## The fix phase that read that reading, the two corrections it made in the record, and the two re-runs it entered and did not repair

*A fix is not a review, it reviewed nothing, it opened no chapter, and it discharges none of the four debts. It is recorded here because continuity is where a later writer looks for what a scene has already been used for, and the two findings below are both about a scene or a line that appears twice.*

**The one chapter number, corrected in four files and named beside the wrong one.** The count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is printed in words at the end of **Chapter 0376**, at `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0376.md:49` — *the count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter was the same before and after, and it is seven* — and **Chapter 0377 prints no count at all**, the only occurrences of the letters *seven* in it being two instances of *seventh* in *the seventh hour*. `state/batch-summary.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md` and `workspace/volume-08/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` all said 0377. **The error was visible inside `state/batch-summary.md` without leaving it, because that file's ten-closings list quotes Chapter 0376's closing in full and the quotation ends with *and it is seven*.** The breach of the batch's own craft instruction is where it was and is unchanged, and the count itself is seven at Chapter 350, 360, 370 and 380 and did not move.

**The two re-runs the batch's own record did not name, entered here and repaired in neither.** *Chapter 0378 against Chapter 0369:* the same lane the width of two doors with the same four feet of it, the same man of about thirty-four with the fair hand, the same woman at the foot of four steps, the same woman of about thirty-five next door at eleven years, the same closing beat — *neither of the two women said one word about the man who had just gone in* — and the two titles both opening on two doors four feet apart. What is new in 0378 is a bill at his door on somebody else's account and three shillings a day at a bar of iron. **The longest shared run between the two files is seventeen words and it is a required dated week, which is why the twenty-word paragraph sweep, the thirty-four-word leave-one-out bound, the thirty-four-word volume-scale variant and the twelve-word duplicate-paragraph test all return a clean figure on a chapter that re-uses a scene.** *Chapter 0374 against Chapter 0361:* one line lifted word for word, *You could send for them*, standalone, in the same counter scene with the same two women nine chapters apart, and the batch's own record of the re-voicings listed 0374 without saying the line was identical. **Neither is repaired, because repairing either means rewriting a chapter, which is the line this project drew for Chapter 0376 against Chapter 0370 and drew it for the right reason. Both are entered as open items, they are the third and fourth instances of item 59, and the standing lesson is that a scene returning to a lane it has already used is a re-run even when every sentence in it is new.**

**And what the fix phase did not do, so that a later reader does not read this section as a repair of prose.** No chapter file was opened, and all ten are byte for byte as the writing phase left them. Nothing in the arrangement was resolved, nobody was thanked, nobody was asked anything, no figure was confirmed or added to, the Lowcross figure is still printed zero times, and the two roads are still walked zero times. The emphasis column and the window columns were **not** re-measured, because the fix phase cannot recover the unit they were counted in, and it printed no third value beside the two that disagree, which is item 62 and is carried. The two errors it did correct were in the record and not in the prose, and the record is the thing this phase was pointed at.

# THE CONTINUITY RECORD OF VOLUME 08 BATCH 0004, CHAPTERS 0381 TO 0390, THE TWO FIGURES THAT CANNOT BE HANDED ON, UNDER PRESSURE FROM THE ORDINARY WEEK

*Appended downward by the run that took `workspace/volume-08/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`. Nothing above this point is rewritten. It is a writing phase's record and not a review of anything.*

## The ten weeks, in one paragraph, and the story of them

**Nobody was asked anything, and the shape of the ten weeks is one person after another getting as far as a piece of paper and stopping.** A book-keeper writes half a sentence at the top of a column he has ruled himself. A clerk of fifty-five does not walk four hundred yards of her own passage. A thing comes out of a drawer and goes back in. A young man takes a sheet off a counter without saying why. A sheet with a heading over a space goes back up four hundred yards of cold flags with the space blank. A form that would move two children stays on a board. A notice with a sum on it goes back into a coat four miles off with the reason unwritten. A man of sixty-one has a pen out for four seconds and does not sign. A form with a heading over a space stays on a hall floor. And a clerk of fifty-five walks to a rented room and tells a man of thirty-eight that the hand which struck his mother's name out of a line is a hand he could name, and he does not write it down, and the name is not printed in the chapter at all.

## The locks, each one named and each one held, and the way it was held

**Nobody asks anybody anything out loud, in ten chapters, in any form. The count is seven before the ten and seven after them, and one question mark is in the batch, in Chapter 0383, in a bare short line in a mouth, about an object, and the two counts are printed separately because a reader with only the first will think a mark is a thing that happened. Nobody is thanked: Chapter 0390 says so out loud twice, once by him and once by her, and the chapter is built on the two of them agreeing not to. Nobody is forgiven, nobody is sent for, nothing is resolved.** The question on the shelf under the pane-less window stays a question with no full stop and her name at the foot, and its author is not asked a second time. The figure at the end of the cold passage gets no heading, is not printed, and the sheet that would have taken it goes back up the passage blank. The four who cannot make sense of a paragraph are not asked one question about a number, and in Chapter 0386 one of the man of about fifty-five with the chain puts his hand flat on the form on the board and says four sentences about the weight of a chain, and the man at the front of the bench reads the form out loud over his hand and nobody notices. A girl of seventeen is not given the chance to say no, because the form has no space for a no and nothing else in that lane has one either. The four hundred and thirty miles and the nine miles and the four hundred yards of cold flags are each walked zero times. The Lowcross figure is named zero times and the form on the hall floor in 0389 is not stated to be the same debt and carries no figure.

**The name at the end of the struck line is not printed in Chapter 0390, and neither is the mother's own name, and the who is not answered.** The person who tells Marek Kest what she tells him says the hand is a hand he could name, and she does not say it a second time, and he does not write it down, and the page does not carry it. A reader holds that the information is in two heads and in neither on paper, and that the man the series is about has wanted it for about two years and has done nothing at all with it. **That is the movement's whole turn and it is a cost and not a resolution, and the next asking is nobody's yet.**

## The card file and the page, and every place the page beat the card

**Ten disagreements, and the page won all ten, which is the only rule this project has ever had about a card.** Chapter 0381's dated week is in the fifth block as the card asked and is in the eighteenth of twenty-one on the page, and the clerk's *no form for the rest of a sentence* is in her mouth unbolded where the card had it emphasised. Chapter 0382's dated week is in the third block as the card asked and is in the nineteenth of twenty-two, and the card's second room is not entered: the page has one room, the shelf and the window and the table, and the passage at the back of the building referred to from four hundred yards off, and the room at the far end of it referred to and not entered. Chapter 0385's field four did not carry Marek Kest and the page does, in a sentence said out loud once to a passage, because the volume outline's floor is three a movement and the card came up one short. Chapter 0387's dated week is in the third block as the card asked and is in the sixteenth of twenty. Chapter 0389's dated week is in the fourth block as the card asked and is in the eighteenth of twenty, and the four minutes that stood in the card's field eight for a typesetter's thinking time did not go on the page, because four minutes is the man of about sixty-one's figure and a figure is not borrowed. **Chapter 0390's card did not name the person who tells him and the page makes it the clerk of about fifty-five, and that is the person the volume's floor and the movement's shape both require, and the card is left as it was written.**

**And where the card was right and the page had to be built to meet it: the ten primary two-word openings are all new against Chapters 001 to 380, the male lead is in three chapters, the count of askings did not move, and the card's calendar is right where the prompt's sentence summarising it was wrong, which is item 56 for the second time in the same place.**

## The scenes this batch stood in that a batch before it had already stood in, entered because a scene is used whether or not a sentence is lifted

**The lane the width of two doors is now in its third chapter, and 0369 and 0378 both used it with the same three people in it, and 0389 is the third.** What is different in 0389 is the staging, and the difference is the point: the woman of about thirty-five next door **knocks on his door, which she has not done twice in eleven years**, where in 0369 she came out on her own step and in 0378 she spoke across four feet without coming down a step. The form is new, the heading is new, and the stopping is new. **The counter at the top of four flights is now in four chapters across two batches and the passage of cold flags is in two, and the ground-floor room off the old river road is in two with the woman of about nineteen in both, and the counting room at the back of a dye end is in three.** The four lifts found and repaired are in `state/batch-summary.md` under this batch's own heading: two lifted beats of sixty-two and sixty-three words in 0386 and 0389 out of closed Chapters 0375 and 0378, an eleven-word duplication inside one paragraph in 0383, and a three-word clause repeated in two consecutive blocks in 0389. **The first two were invisible to every sweep in the standing list, and that is the fourth and fifth instances of item 59 and the reason the last-six sweep exists.**

**Everything the page was given in this batch came out as the page was given it except two things, and both were caught before this record was written.** *A four minutes in Chapter 0389* was standing in for the time a typesetter took over a heading, and four minutes is the man of about sixty-one's want and is not a thing a chapter borrows; it is now *a few minutes*, twice. *Four houses off the lane* in Chapter 0387 misstated the fourth house, which is one house; it is now *the fourth house off that lane*. **Nothing else moved: three shillings and a shilling are not on the page in this batch, fourpence is on it three times and all three in Chapter 0390, nineteen days of a crew's money is on it once, the second-hand-since-forty-two line is on it once, the wage-held date is on it once, the seventh-month box date is on it once, and the Lowcross figure is on it zero times.** The count of askings is seven and is not printed in any of the ten files. The numeral is printed zero times. The chapter count of the count is not printed. The names at the feet of two sheets are not printed. The figure at the end of the cold passage is not printed. The number in a man's head is not printed.

## The four debts, restated in full at Chapter 390, and this phase discharges none of them

**An owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, four volumes on. Owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's own Batch 0003, which is four and not three. The review gate, which opened and produced findings on all seven Batch 0005 phases the commit history records, and has fallen back to the writing agent every time it has been asked, and has never once produced a review that could be certified independent, and a gate that only ever falls back is not a gate that passed either. And the open items that are not plot, with the sentence bound among them and the unit columns among them.** `state/phase-ledger.json` reads `phase-000-bootstrap` and `planned` at more than twenty phases out of date, no phase may edit it, and it is carried as item 43. The number of debts has not moved in eight volumes and the number of phases that have claimed to discharge one is zero, and this section is a record and not a discharge.

## The measurements this phase re-measured rather than inherited, and what re-measuring found

**The emphasis columns of Batch 0003 were re-measured from its ten files and both of them reproduce exactly, and that is the finding.** The reading is one sentence and it is the one the record had been quoting wrongly: the numerator is the characters inside paired `**` runs with the markers and the internal whitespace inside a run both excluded, and the two denominators are the non-whitespace characters of the prose lines for joint two and every character of a prose line including the line ends for joint one. On that reading Batch 0003's printed columns are joint two 11.51 / 14.10 / 10.67 / 11.77 / 6.29 / 10.94 / 12.42 / 10.48 / 15.51 / 15.83 and joint one 9.05 / 11.15 / 8.45 / 9.35 / 4.98 / 8.67 / 9.79 / 8.29 / 12.30 / 12.54, and both are what `state/current.md` and `state/batch-summary.md` print. **The value this record does not print beside the one it does is item 57's range of 8.04 to 15.81, which is not reproducible from the files under either denominator, and the reading quoted in three prompts — internal whitespace inside a run counted — is not the reading that produced any column in the record. That is item 63, and it is the first time in this project that a measure was found to be right in the files and wrong in every place it was written down, and it took four batches to find because nobody re-measured the batch the measure was invented on.**

**The sentence bound's pinned reading was verified against Batch 0003's printed column before it was used and reproduces it to the word, and the closed-volume word rows and all eight checkpoints were re-derived one file at a time rather than inherited, and they hold.** **Batch 0003's fifteen-word overlapping-window column, by contrast, does not reproduce from its files under any of the four readings tested, and the one reading that matches six of its ten figures is a function-word removal whose stopword list is not written down anywhere, which means the reading cannot be recovered and only bounded. It is carried as stale and is item 64, and the rule that comes out of it is that a sweep whose token filter is not in the record is not a sweep a later phase can reproduce, and the filter is as much a part of the method as the window size.**

### The card against the page, and the page won every disagreement, which is item 60 and the standing practice

**The card file `outline/batches/volume-08-batch-0004.md` was written by the same run that wrote the prose, in the same commit, and is a record of intent. It is not rewritten.** The disagreements between it and the page, all of which the page settles: **field seven, the ten `no form` openings, is wrong about Chapter 0383, whose first opening on the page is *no form for a fact two people share about a building* and not *no form for a thing taken out of a drawer*; about Chapter 0388, whose two openings on the page are *no form for a page and I do not want one* and *no form for a basket that does not come back up* and not *no form for four seconds with a pen out*; and about Chapter 0390, which carries the frame once, in her mouth, and not twice.** **Field four for 0385 did not carry Marek Kest and the page does.** **The card's claim that all ten of its two-word primary openings return nothing over Chapters 001 to 380 is a false zero, and it is a false zero about the manuscript rather than about these ten files: *a thing*, *a book*, *a name*, *a form*, *a word* and *four seconds* each return hundreds of times across the manuscript as ordinary words, and what the sweep is for is the narrower question of whether they are the opening of the frame, and on that narrower question all ten return nothing outside this batch.** **And one field the card got right for the wrong reason, which is worth printing because it is the case the standing rule exists for: the card gave 0390's first opening as *no form for a name a person could name*, and that is a two-word opening the page has never used, and the page did not use it, and the reason is that the chapter's strongest moment is the hand and not the name, and a writer who had been told the opening in advance would have built a sentence around a phrase instead of around a scene.** The card file is left exactly as it stands.

## VOLUME 08, BATCH 0005, CHAPTERS 0391 TO 0400 — CONTINUITY, WRITTEN BY THE WRITING PHASE THAT WROTE THEM

**This section was written by the run that wrote the ten chapters, after the last of its edits to the page, and it is a state record and not a review: it reviewed nothing and it discharges none of the four debts. Nothing above this point is rewritten.**

**The ten weeks, and what each of them is.** Three hundred and ninety chapters stood open and this batch took the manuscript to four hundred. Two weeks in the eleventh month, four in the twelfth, four in the first month of the next named year, and two plus four plus four is ten, and the day inside the week is the inherited cycle of second, second, fourth, fourth, restarting with each month. **Chapter 0400 is the last chapter of Volume 08 and nothing closes the volume; the close is owed to a phase and a close is not a review.**

**The year names, and the decision this batch took, recorded here because the prompt left it to the writing phase.** There are five names and none is re-based or re-derived. The sixth is the fifth with one more *after the* in it and it is *the year after the year after the year after the year after next*; Chapters 0391 to 0396 stand in it. **The seventh is the sixth with one more *after the* in it — the year after the year after the year after the year after the year after next — added on the rule and not moved, written out in full at every occurrence and abbreviated nowhere, and it is carried by Chapters 0397 to 0400, four files, one occurrence each. The decision to write it out and where to record it was this writing phase's and it is taken here, in this section, in `state/open-threads.md` and in the batch's closing record.**

**What moved and what did not.** The count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is seven, and it is seven at Chapter 350, 360, 370, 380, 390 and 400, and it is printed in none of these ten files and no figure for it is printed in any of them in words or in a numeral. **Nobody was thanked, nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for, and nothing was resolved.** The two people in their boxes stay in them and the form that put them there is not asked to be void. The question on the shelf under a window with no pane in it stays a question with no full stop and its author is not asked a second time. The figure at the end of the cold passage is not handed on, does not get a heading, and is not printed in any form. **The name at the end of a struck line is not printed in any of these ten files, and the name of the hand that struck it is not printed in any of them, and nothing in this batch turns what the clerk of about fifty-five said in Chapter 0390 into a thing anybody can be served with.** The guarantee is not printed in its own words and no child is named in it. **The bill at Lowcross stands at nineteen pounds three and fourpence, unpaid, nobody liable, and this batch named it zero times, which is the fifth batch in a row and is a decision and not an oversight; the form that came through a letter slot in Chapter 0389 is a different thing, carries no figure, is on nobody's account the page names, and no chapter of this batch says the two are the same debt.** The four who cannot make sense of a paragraph are not asked one question about a number, and the man of about fifty-five with the chain and the man of about sixty-one are neither of them one of them. A girl of seventeen and a reader of seventeen are unspoken to, unthanked, unsent for and not going to start, and the girl was not given the chance to say no. **The four hundred and thirty miles, the nine miles and the four hundred yards of cold flags are each walked zero times in these ten chapters, and the volume's own count of each is zero and was zero in the four batches before it.** The two documents stay two documents with no instrument joining them. The quarterly return does not appear and is in no reply. The four appearances of the substituted wording keep their four ages and this batch adds no fifth. The three stitches stay three things. The two sealed pages stay shut. The counting-house mark is a mark and no chapter converts it. Forty-four thousand eight hundred and nineteen is not printed. The Venn ruling has five operative terms and is never said to have four, and its schedule is referred to zero times.

**The ten closings are ten shapes and no two of them share one.** A box three inches forward of where six years of nobody put it, with nobody at that counter saying a word. A window corner that is warm, and a woman who did not put her hands in her sleeves at the door. Stone that is as cold at the end of a week as it was at the start of it. A book with her name on the head of it, unopened, and the borrowed ones going back where they came from. A paper that will be up on a board again next week, and nobody who has said a word to either of the two at the back. A basket hook that is empty, and a man who went to bed without taking the pen out. A woman who went back four feet after a chair moved in a hall she has never entered. A shut box, a lane not walked, and nobody who could be asked about either. A man who has not been talked out of anything because nobody said a word to him and she said all the rest. **And the last line of the volume, which is that nothing is asked of anybody and the arrangement is still standing.**

**The male lead, and the floor, and the honest count.** `outline/volume-08.md` sets a floor of three chapters a movement, in person or as a person whose absence has a named cost in the room he is not in. **He is on the page in three of these ten: 0392, where a clerk of about fifty-five states that a man of thirty-eight walked the whole of the distance to the back of her building once and has not been in that building since and that what she said out loud at the other end of it cannot be said again or not said again; 0398, where the clerk of about twenty-nine says out loud that the man of thirty-eight who audits contracts for an empire is the only person anybody knows of who could walk down that lane without it being a record of it, and that he is not in that building and nobody there knows where he is this week, and that the four hundred yards is not walked; and 0399, in person, in a rented room, with his want on the page in his own hand and in his own voice.** **The floor is met at three and not exceeded, and the two absences are absences with a cost named in somebody else's mouth and not descriptions of him, and neither of them required an invention.** His want is visible in 0399 and is named as the reason a lane is not walked in 0398. He resolves nothing, he is not thanked, nobody asks him anything, and the woman about four miles off at the top of a stair is still not told that he said a reason out loud to about nine men in a shed.

**What a reader inherits at Chapter 400, in one paragraph, for the volume-close phase.** A box of about four hundred bought and unfilled blanks stands three inches further forward than it stood ten weeks ago and has not been opened in six years. A form has been on a board at the Slade end since the tenth month and will be on it again on the second of the two days next week, and the two of seventeen at the back of that shed are still on that bench and have still not been spoken to. A woman's undertaking of the fourth of the first month is live, unretired and unexercised, and a woman at a counter is the only other person who knows she is ready and has told her so. Nineteen days of a crew's money are in a coat four miles off with a notice on the back of it whose printed space is clean. A rule said once in a lane lives in the woman who heard it. A man at the foot of about nine hundred sheets a year wants about four minutes he has wanted for about nineteen years and is not getting them, and a young man of twenty-six who is paid by a corner has said out loud that a second true thing is coming in about four months and is paid for nothing else. A figure at the end of a cold passage has not gone down and is on no paper. A number in a man's head is on no paper and the paper with a printed heading over a ruled space is gone from a hall floor and nobody in that lane knows who took it. A woman who is next door has knocked twice in eleven years and is now responsible for not knocking a third time. A book-keeper wants the rest of a sentence and has not asked. A name that is in two heads is on no paper at all. **And nothing was asked, and nothing was thanked, and nothing was forgiven, and nothing was sent for, and nothing was resolved.**

## VOLUME 08, BATCH 0005, CHAPTERS 0391 TO 0400 — CONTINUITY OF THE REPAIR THAT WAS APPLIED TO THEM, AND NOT A CLOSE

**This section is written by the repair phase that applied the first reading of Batch 0005, which is `reviews/volume-08-batch-0005.md`. It is a repair record and not a review, not a self-audit and not a close, and it discharges none of the four debts. Five chapters were touched: 0391, 0392, 0397, 0399 and 0400. Sixty words. No event moved, no date in the calendar moved, the count of askings did not move, and no question mark entered any of the ten files.**

### The four people the state layer keeps apart at one address after this repair, and why a merge there went nine volumes without a rule catching it

**The clerk of about fifty-five** keeps minutes in a room on the ground floor of a building off the old river road, about four hundred yards from the rented room she walked the whole distance to in Chapter 0390, and has been in that room nineteen years, and her own question is still on a shelf under a window with no pane in it with no full stop on the end of it. **The woman who keeps the ground floor of the house Marek Kest rents** has been at the bottom of that house's stairs about twenty years, has never once been above the second one, takes his rent on one day in the week, and has never had his name and has never given him hers. **Marek Kest** is the third of the three and holds nothing, and in Chapter 0399 he is the only one of the three who has given a thing out loud. Chapter 0399's first draft had the second of the three saying she was the one who put the first one's thing where it is and had not slept properly since she did it, which gave a woman who has been in a house about twenty years a confession made in a room about four hundred yards off, and the sentence that introduced the clerk nine weeks earlier said that clerk had not come back and was not going to. **The chapter now separates them in the paragraph that introduces the ground-floor woman, in the volume's own manner and without a word of narration about repairs, and the claim in her mouth is a claim about her own carrying.** Item 79 carries the rest.

### The four intervals, measured against the calendar and not estimated, and the two that were right

**A month in this calendar is four weeks, the day inside the week is second, second, fourth, fourth, restarting with each month, and the step from one chapter to the next is seven days.** Chapter 0381 is the second day of the first week of the ninth month of the sixth named year and Chapter 0400 is the fourth day of the fourth week of the first month of the seventh, and every position in between follows from those two and reproduces on the page. Measured from it: **Chapter 0392 said a week and it is two. Chapter 0399 said a week and it is nine, and nine is the distance from Chapter 0390, where the clerk came, and the ninth week of the volume's last movement. Chapter 0400 said four weeks and it is seventeen, and seventeen is the distance from Chapter 0383, where the fourth of the four things came out onto that counter at the fourth hour. Chapter 0397 said a fortnight and it is eight, and eight is the distance from Chapter 0389, where the woman next door knocked.** Two figures in the batch were already right and were left alone: Chapter 0395's *four weeks ago* and *three weeks ago* for the writing that went up a shed and came back, which are weeks this volume does not depict, and Chapter 0396's *a week ago* for a pen in a pot, which is Chapter 0395's week and is a corridor nine miles off a building off the old river road and not that shed.

**One interval in the batch does not reconcile and was not repaired.** Chapter 0391 puts a woman at that counter's third thing said out loud to her colleague a fortnight before it, and Chapter 0400 puts the same third thing about a month before that, and the two are eight weeks apart. Neither week is depicted, so neither figure can be derived from the page. Carried as item 83 with both readings named, and a phase that picks one has made a guess and written it down as a repair.

### The two sets of inches, and what happens to a figure that is copied before it is checked

**Chapter 0391 moves a box four inches along a counter because a woman wanted a length of twine from behind it, and then sets it down an inch short of where it had been, so that the two distances are both on the counter and neither of them has to be believed.** Four inches out and one inch back is three inches, and the chapter said a quarter of an inch in three places and the state layer had copied the wrong one into four files. **The page now says three inches in all three, and Chapter 0400's standing description of the box says three inches further forward than it stood ten weeks ago, which is the distance between the two chapters, and the *three months* that stood there has gone because a month in this calendar is four weeks and ten weeks is not three of them.** The volume's craft rule is that `about` is kept where the imprecision is the point, and no figure on this page is a case where it is.

### What this repair did not do, entered so a later reader does not read this section as a plot change

**No box is opened, no coat is emptied, no ruled space is filled in, no form is drawn, no question is asked, nobody is thanked, nothing is sent for, nothing is resolved, and the arrangement is still standing.** The count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is seven and it was seven at Chapter 350. **The woman of twenty is still in a counting room about four hundred and thirty miles up this river and is still not going to ask what her wage is held against. The foreman of fifty-one is still in a box entered by a class. The man of about sixty-one is still the second hand and has still not taken the pen out twice in a week. The undertaking of the fourth of the first month is live, unretired and unexercised, and its date is not said out loud and is not said out loud here.** **The name of the hand that struck the line out of the leaf in his coat is not printed in this section, and the who is Volume 06's, and it goes on unanswered, and the second half of the question is not asked.**

### The five figures in the measurements that this repair did and did not reproduce

**Re-measured after the last edit and identical: the word counts, the question marks, the twelve month names, the seven weekday names, the modal verb *may*, *schedule*, *register*, *this year*, the `no form` frame at 2 / 3 / 2 / 2 / 2 / 2 / 3 / 4 / 2 / 2 and twenty-four in all, `about nine` in both readings at eight and five, and the thirty-four-word intra-batch and volume-scale bounds at 0 in all ten.** **Re-measured and moved: the per-file words, the batch total, the manuscript total, the `about` count at a hundred and seventeen against a hundred and fifteen, and the per-file maximum sentence, where the reading reproduces the record on six of ten files before any edit, reads three one word lower for reasons in the splitter rather than on the page, and where Chapter 0391 falls from 87 to 84 because the repair took a four-word phrase out of the middle of that chapter's longest sentence three times; the governing maximum of 88 does not move.** **Not reproduced and therefore not overwritten: the fifteen, thirteen and twenty-word shared-run columns, and the before-and-after check that bears on a repair ran one derived reading over all five edited chapters at seven window sizes and returned an identical cell in every one of them.** Item 85 carries it, and the blind spot is written beside it: **nothing in this project's check list can see a calendar, and five intervals were wrong in a batch where every other figure was right.**

## THE CLOSE OF VOLUME 08 AT CHAPTER 0400, AND NOTHING ABOVE THIS POINT IS REWRITTEN

**This section was written by the close phase that took `workspace/volume-08/volume-close/PROMPT.md`, after its last edit to anything in this repository. It is a close and it is not a review, not a self-audit and not a second reading; it reviewed nothing, and it discharges none of the four debts. No chapter of Volume 08 was opened to change a word of it and no chapter of any closed volume was opened at all. `outline/series.md` is not touched, `outline/ending.md` is not opened for edit, and the ending of record is still Volume 12 and the final image is still an ordinary local hearing.**

### The figures, with the method named before each, and the blind spot written beside each

**Words, per file, `sed 's/[[:space:]]*$//' file | wc -w`, one file at a time and never with a glob, summed per file, measured by this close and not inherited: Volume 08's five movements at 15,506 / 11,208 / 11,365 / 12,472 / 12,594, the volume at fifty files and 63,145, the manuscript at four hundred files and 1,091,877, and everything through Chapter 390 at 1,079,283.** The seven closed volumes reproduce to the word at 202,117 / 184,039 / 176,097 / 148,821 / 144,248 / 101,261 / 72,149, and the close ran that before anything else because it is the check that says the method is this project's own method. The checkpoints, measured cumulatively on the same method, are 956,583 / 987,184 / 1,001,358 / 1,015,936 / 1,028,732 / 1,044,238 / 1,055,446 / 1,066,811 / 1,079,283 and 1,091,877 at Chapters 300 / 320 / 330 / 340 / 350 / 360 / 370 / 380 / 390 / 400, and the last of them equals the manuscript, which is the check that says this close opened no closed chapter. **The figures these supersede are named and not deleted: 63,085 for the volume and 1,091,817 and 1,091,806 for the manuscript, all three taken before the repair that applied the first reading of Batch 0005 or before the writing phase's last edit to the page, and the repair changed 60 words across five chapters.** The blind spot: a word count cannot see whether the words are the same words, and a per-file sum and a concatenation of the same files differ by one word for each file that does not end in a newline, which is item 58.

**The date lines, read out of the fifty files by counting the year names and the week numbers, and this is the one instrument this close added to the list because nothing in this project had it: forty-nine of the fifty chapters carry a dated week line and Chapter 0379 carries none.** The sixth named year is written out in full at every occurrence in Chapters 0351 to 0396, being the sixth name with one more *after the* in it, and the seventh named year is the sixth with one more again, written out in full once in each of Chapters 0397, 0398, 0399 and 0400 and nowhere in the three hundred and ninety-six chapters before it, so the addition is clean and the name was added and not moved. **What the date lines do not add up to is the year.** Counting the weeks the page actually prints, the second month of the sixth named year is three weeks long — Chapter 0355 is its first week, Chapter 0356 prints its third and Chapter 0357 its fourth — and the third month is three weeks long, because Chapter 0360 prints the third week of the third month and Chapter 0361 opens the fourth month. **The sixth named year therefore holds forty-six weeks and not the forty-eight the calendar gives it, and is two weeks short of it, and fifty chapters at one week each do not fit in forty-eight. Forty-five files print a date line and Chapter 0379 prints none, and a chapter is a week whether or not it dates itself, so the undated one is a defect in the printing and not a week out of the year — it stands between Chapter 0378's second week of the eighth month and Chapter 0380's fourth, which makes the eighth month an ordinary four.** The consequence for the year name is that the seventh named year begins at Chapter 0397 where `outline/volume-08.md` predicted Chapter 0399, and the name was still added and not moved and is still right; the week it lands on is a different thing from the rule. **This close repaired none of it, and the reason is the standing one: a close writes no prose, and a repair that chooses a week is a guess.** Carried as item 86, and the outline's own Movement Five week arithmetic is carried as item 88. **The close's first printing of this paragraph said *forty-six dated weeks*, and the forty-sixth is the one that is not dated, and it put the undated chapter among the short months and so made the year's shortfall read as three; the two weeks are the two months. Both wrong forms are named and neither is deleted.** The blind spot beside it: this project measures sentence length, emphasis, word counts, lifted phrases and the count of askings, and has never measured a calendar, and the calendar is the only number in the volume a reader can check with a pencil.

**The male-lead floor, counted from the fifty files by finding every chapter in which the protagonist is in person or is an absence with a cost named in somebody else's mouth, which is `outline/volume-08.md`'s own definition of the count: 1 / 1 / 3 / 3 / 3 across the five movements, eleven chapters of fifty, and the chapters are 0355; 0365; 0371, 0377 and 0379; 0382, 0385 and 0390; and 0392, 0398 and 0399.** The floor is three a movement. It is met in the last three movements and missed in the first two, and his want is visible in at least one chapter of every one of the five, which is the second half of the same floor. **The close's own brief stated that the volume met the floor in Movement Five at three and in neither of the two before it, and that is wrong for Movement Four, which met it at three, and the measured column is printed above with the wrong one named beside it and not deleted.** Carried as item 87. The blind spot: a count of chapters cannot see whether a protagonist in a room is a protagonist in a scene, which is the finding the floor arrived with and which a count of three does not answer.

**The sweeps this close ran over the volume's fifty files, with the method beside each: question marks, 4, in Chapters 0364, 0373, 0375 and 0383, one in each, and not one of the four is an asking.** The twelve month names and the seven weekday names are 0 in both forms and the modal verb *may* is 0; *this year*, *schedule* and *register* are 0; the count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is **seven**, and it is the same figure at Chapter 350 and at Chapter 400, and no chapter of the fifty printed it in words or in a numeral, and the next asking is nobody's yet and this volume did not make it anybody's. The name of the woman whose name this volume does not print appears in none of the fifty files, and neither does the name of the hand that struck the line out of the leaf in a rented coat. The bill at Lowcross is named in none of the fifty files, which is five batches in a row and is a decision and not an oversight, and the form that came through a letter slot in Chapter 0389 is a different thing that carries no figure. **The blind spot of every one of these: a count cannot see a mouth and cannot see an asking, and a string sweep against a corpus sees a formula and a re-print as the same thing, which is item 76, and a count of a name tells a reader the name is absent without telling them what is in its place.**

### The people at the end of the volume, and the thing each of them is still refused

**Marek Kest, thirty-eight.** He wants the erased name of his mother and the disputed family claim and has neither, and the name of the hand that struck the line out of that leaf is in his head and on no paper and is printed in no chapter of this volume. He holds nothing, refuses the reading, does not exercise the thing he is able to do, and is not at a stage. He is not thanked, nobody asked him anything, and he asked nobody anything. **A woman who keeps the ground floor of the house he rents told him in Chapter 0399 that a person who knows a thing and does not write it down has to be the person who remembers it, and that he is one of two people in this empire keeping a thing in that condition, and that the other one is her; and she is not the clerk of about fifty-five who came the whole of that distance in Chapter 0390, and the chapter now says so in the paragraph that introduces her, and she has never been inside that building and does not know what was said in it.** That separation is item 79 and it is the one this volume broke in a building nobody had thought to check.

**Tamsin Rook, twenty-four.** Nobody has ever put a question to her in two years, and in the last chapter a colleague told her out loud that a person who has been ready for two years has made herself the only thing in that room that can be said something to, and that readiness is not a form and cannot be entered on anything, and that the day somebody at the top of those four flights finds out is the day she gets used. **She did not exercise the undertaking, did not retire it, and did not say the date out loud, and she has not said it out loud in two years. She did one smaller thing, at a quarter past the fourth hour: she took her apron off the hook and hung it up again, because she had come in twenty minutes early for two years and there had been no need to.** The last line of the volume is that nothing is asked of anybody.

**Nell Kest, twenty, about four hundred and thirty miles up this river.** Sixteen volumes on that bench, fifteen borrowed, one with her own name in capitals at the head of it. Nineteen days of a crew's money in a coat four miles off in a room she has not stood in since she was seventeen, and a notice with a printed ruled space on the back of it in the same pocket with the space clean. The coat went four miles and came back four miles and the money is the same. A rule said once in a lane lives in her and not in the woman who said it, and the second of the four women has said so out loud and is not thanked and is not forgiven and asks for nothing. **She is not going up this river this season and a season is not a life.**

**Halla Wray, fifty-one.** Still in a box entered by a class, and the form that would take the two of seventeen at the back of that shed out of the back of it is still on the board and will be on it again on the second of the two days next week, and no one in that lane has the standing to find anything void, because nothing in this empire has ever been asked to be void and there is no procedure for asking. The girl is not given the chance to say no. The man of about fifty-five with the chain said his four sentences and nobody answered him and he never wanted an answer. The second half of a sentence she began in a doorway has still not arrived.

**Rennick Adley, thirty-eight.** Wants the rest of a sentence and has not asked and is not going to, and he wrote the same four lines out for the eleventh time and they came out the same. The clerk of about twenty-nine told him out loud that a box entered by a class does not come out of itself, and that there is nobody he could tell and no form for telling and no standing for telling, and that the not telling is not a decision he made this week. The four hundred yards is not walked.

**The clerk of about fifty-five.** Nineteen years in one room off the old river road, writes the minutes, advises nobody, asks nothing, and her own finding is on a shelf under a window with no pane in it: a question with no full stop and her own name at the foot, and the only thing on that shelf with a name at the foot. **In Chapter 0392, the first time she is on the page in this volume with nothing to withhold, she said one true thing out loud in that room that is not about the sheets, and it was about the weather, and the woman of about nineteen wrote none of it down and has said nothing about it since.** She is the only person in this matter anybody could serve a question on in writing, and her own question stays a question with no full stop and nobody has asked her a second time.

**The man of about sixty-one.** Signs at the foot of about nine hundred sheets a year and cannot read a paragraph, and about nine of them a year are not what the person above him wrote and he signed every one, and he is the only person alive who knows which. A young man of about twenty-six paid by the corner told him out loud that the second true thing will come out in about four months and that the corridor with the hook in the wall is the only place it will happen. He answered once, to the pen pot. It did not arrive this week, his own figure is neither confirmed nor denied, and the nine miles is not walked.

**The man of about thirty-four with the fair hand.** The only person anybody could put a question to and the one nobody has, with a number in his head that has never been written and never passed on. He told the woman next door in Chapter 0397 that there is no form for the second knock of the two and that she has made herself responsible for the not knocking, and that he has put it on her because he has nobody he could hand it to. He filled in nothing. The woman next door is now answerable for not knocking a third time, out loud, in a lane, and there is no form for that.

**The woman of about twenty-six at the end of about four hundred yards of cold flags.** Keeps a figure in her head that is not written down, is not handed on, has gone up and has not gone down, and does not get a heading. The room at the far end is not entered and the four hundred yards is not walked by anybody in the closing movement.

**The three the state layer keeps apart, kept apart, and a fourth separation this volume added.** A man of about sixty-one is nine miles off a building off the old river road and is neither of the two women in their boxes; a woman of about twenty-six is at the end of a cold passage nine miles off the lane off the Slade and is a third person; a woman of twenty is about four hundred and thirty miles up this river in a furnace town. **And the woman who keeps the ground floor of a rented house is not the clerk of about fifty-five: the clerk keeps minutes about four hundred yards off the old river road and has been in that room nineteen years, and the other has been at the bottom of a stranger's stairs about twenty years and takes his rent. Chapter 0399's first draft had the second saying she was the one who put the first's thing where it is, which merged two people this state layer has kept apart for eight volumes; the chapter is repaired and the separation is on the page in the paragraph that introduces her.**

### What the volume hands on, in the present tense, and what it resolved

**Nobody was thanked, nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for, and nothing was resolved, in fifty chapters.** The practice in two people about four hundred and thirty miles apart continues with no list, no form, no heading and no fund, and neither has said the other's name out loud. The four hundred and thirty miles is walked zero times, the nine miles is walked zero times, and the four hundred yards of cold flags is not walked by anybody in the closing movement. The question in the second of the eleven books is still a question, carries no period, and its author is not asked a second time. The guarantee is not printed in its own words and no child is named in it, and the four who cannot make sense of a paragraph are not asked one question about a number, and the man of about fifty-five with the chain and the man of about sixty-one are neither of them one of the four. A girl of seventeen and a reader of seventeen are unspoken to, unthanked and unsent for, and are not going to start. **The male lead's reason for not reaching a stage is in about nine words on a page and is not paid a second time, and his want is not resolved, and the name of the hand that struck the line is not printed, and the who is Volume 06's and goes on unanswered.**

**The volume's own finding is that the cost of a no is a number of people, and one of them is the man the series is about. The next asking is nobody's yet and this close does not make it anybody's, and a close that treated a woman being ready as a plot turn would have written a different book.** The four debts are restated in full in the newest section of `state/open-threads.md` and in the newest two of `state/character-state.md`, and in the fifth section of this close, and this close discharges none of them; there is no Volume 09 section in this file, because the brief gives Volume 09 sections to two files and this is not one of them.
