OPEN, ADDED BY THIS PHASE, AND IT IS ABOUT THIS PROJECT'S HABIT OF RE-USING ITS OWN ROOMS, AND IT IS A CARD INSTRUCTION THAT WAS NOT CARRIED OUT.** `outline/volume-08.md` and the batch prompt both say a room is described once and referred to afterwards, and **all eight of the ten rooms in this batch are referred to and not described, and the counter at the top of four flights — which the batch prompt planned to be described once in 0391 and referred to in 0400 — was referred to in both and not described in either, and the tenth chapter of the volume opens on a drawer, a box and a hundred feet of floor instead of on a room.** That is a card correction and the page governed: the card's field two for 0400 said *referred to and not described again* and its preamble said the counter was described in 0391, and the page did neither thing and the card was wrong in both places in the same direction. **The finding is that a card that plans a description is planning the exact damage item 76 measures, because a description is the paragraph a later chapter will find twenty words of itself inside, and a rule about rooms is worth more enforced in the card file than stated in the outline.** Payoff target: the card file of Volume 09's first batch, and the card file template.

## The four debts, restated at Chapter 400, and this phase discharges none of them

**One: an owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, four volumes on. Two, three and four: owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's own Batch 0003, which is four and not three. Plus the review gate, which opened and produced findings on all seven Batch 0005 phases the commit history records, and has fallen back to the writing agent every time it has been asked, and has never once produced a review that could be certified independent, and a gate that only ever falls back is not a gate that passed either. And the open items that are not plot, with the sentence bound among them, and items 43, 63, 70 and 71 among them, and `state/phase-ledger.json` among them, which no phase may edit, which reads `phase-000-bootstrap` and `planned`, and which is carried and not repaired.** The number of debts has not moved in eight volumes and the number of phases that have claimed to discharge one is zero, and this section writes four new items into the ledger and discharges nothing. **A batch that closes a volume is not a close of the volume, and a close is not a review, and this phase is neither.**

## THE REPAIR OF BATCH 0005 AT CHAPTER 400, SEVEN ITEMS ADDED TO THE LEDGER, AND NOT ONE OF THEM IS A PLOT THREAD

**This section was written by the repair phase that applied the first reading of Batch 0005, which is `reviews/volume-08-batch-0005.md`. It is a repair record and it is not a review, it is not a self-audit and it is not a close, and it discharges none of the four debts. Five chapters were touched, 60 words, no event and no date moved, and the count of askings did not move.**

**79. OPEN, ADDED BY THIS REPAIR, AND IT IS ITEM 29 BREAKING A NINTH TIME, IN A BUILDING NOBODY HAD THOUGHT TO CHECK.** Chapter 0399's seventh paragraph said the clerk who came the whole of that distance had not come back and was not going to, and its twenty-seventh had the woman who keeps the ground floor of the rented house say she was the one who put his thing there and had not slept properly since she did it. **That merged the clerk of about fifty-five, who keeps minutes nineteen years in a room about four hundred yards off the old river road, with a woman who has been at the bottom of another man's stairs about twenty years and takes his rent, and the state layer has kept those two apart for eight volumes and had never had a lock on the pair because it never had a rule about them.** **The chapter now says in the paragraph that introduces her that she is not the woman who came, and the claim in her mouth is a claim about her own carrying and not about his, and three state files that carried the merge as settled canon are corrected with the wrong wording named beside the right one.** The rule that comes out of it is item 29's, unchanged and now with a ninth instance and a new class of site: **a merge does not need two rooms to happen. It needs two people and one mouth, and the second mouth in this case was the ground floor of a house nobody had thought of as part of this matter at all.** Payoff target: the standing separation rule, which lists three people and does not list this one, and the first card of Volume 09.

**80. OPEN, ADDED BY THIS REPAIR, AND IT IS THE FINDING UNDER THE FINDING, AND IT IS THE FIRST UNMEASURED INSTRUMENT THIS PROJECT HAS NAMED.** **Four intervals in the ten chapters of Batch 0005 were wrong against the volume's own calendar: Chapter 0392 said a week where two weeks stand, Chapter 0399 said a week where nine stand, Chapter 0400 said four weeks where seventeen stand, and Chapter 0397 — which the review did not flag — said a fortnight where eight stand.** All four are corrected. **The reason they were all wrong and the reason nothing caught them is the same sentence: this project measures sentence length, emphasis, word counts, lifted phrases and the count of askings, and has never once measured an interval against the calendar, and the calendar is the only number in the volume a reader can check with a pencil.** The rule that comes out of it, and it is the standing instruction for Volume 09's first batch: **a month is four weeks; the day inside the week is second, second, fourth, fourth, restarting with each month; every interval stated in a chapter is a seven-day step from the chapter before it unless the chapter says otherwise in figures; and the interval is measured before the sentence is written and not corrected after.** Payoff target: the standing check list, where a calendar cross-check has to appear as a named check with a named method, and the card template, which has a field for the day and has never had one for the interval back to the previous chapter.

**81. OPEN, ADDED BY THIS REPAIR, AND IT IS ABOUT THE PIPELINE AND NOT ABOUT THE BOOK, AND IT COST A VOLUME ITS NEXT PHASE FOR ONE RUN.** **The writing phase of Batch 0005 closed Volume 08, wrote into `state/current.md` that it had created no next prompt, and stopped, and a sentence in a state file is not an artifact.** `scripts/novel_runner.sh` selects a phase by walking for `PROMPT.md` in directories that hold no terminal marker, so there was nothing for the walk to dispatch on and the volume ended with a full stop in prose and an empty queue. `workspace/volume-08/volume-close/PROMPT.md` now exists and is the only next phase. The rule that comes out of it is the oldest one in this project and had to be restated because it was broken anyway: **a handoff is a file and not a statement about a handoff, and a phase that writes its own successor's prompt is the only phase that can guarantee the run after it happens.** Payoff target: every phase prompt in `workspace/`, and the runner's own walk.

**82. OPEN, ADDED BY THIS REPAIR, AND IT IS THE SAME DEBT AS ITEM 43 AND IT HAS NOW BEEN ASKED EIGHT TIMES.** **The review of Batch 0005 reached the repair as a run log, `logs/` is gitignored, and `reviews/` held no file for it; the review is now at `reviews/volume-08-batch-0005.md`, written by the run that applied it, which discharges the requirement in form and not in fact.** The gate printed `agent "novel-reviewer" is a subagent, not a primary agent. Falling back to default agent` again, and the run that produced the findings was asked for findings with no instruction to be agreeable, which is closer to independence than the fallback has come in eight attempts and is still not certified, because the run that inherited the log is not the run that wrote it. **Not one of the eight entries in that register can be certified independent, and the phrase this project already uses for a register in that condition is the right one.** Payoff target: the gate itself, and the ledger that should have identified the reviewing run and is item 43.

**83. OPEN, ADDED BY THIS REPAIR, AND IT IS A DEFECT THIS REPAIR DELIBERATELY DID NOT REPAIR, AND BOTH FIGURES ARE NAMED SO THAT A LATER PHASE DOES NOT PICK ONE BY GUESS.** **Chapter 0391 says a woman at that counter said a third thing out loud to her colleague a fortnight ago, and Chapter 0400 says she has said a third thing out loud to her in about a month. Those are eight weeks apart.** Neither week is depicted in any chapter of the volume, so neither figure can be derived from the page, and choosing between two loose recollections with nothing on the page to decide between them is how items 25 and 31 of this project were made. **The defect is carried unrepaired and the two readings stand as they are, and a close that reconciles them by picking one has written a repair that is a guess.** Payoff target: nobody, until a chapter depicts one of those weeks and settles it.

**84. OPEN, ADDED BY THIS REPAIR, AND IT IS CARRIED AND NOT ACTED ON, AND THE REASON IS THE SAME REASON ITEM 83'S IS.** The five rolling state files are append-only and large — `continuity.md` and `batch-summary.md` above a quarter of a megabyte each, `character-state.md` and `open-threads.md` above a hundred and eighty kilobytes each — and `state/archive/current-md-superseded.md` is past a thousand lines, and AGENTS.md asks for records *compact and useful for the next batch*, and `outline/volume-08.md`'s own eighth craft rule names the remedy and says *this volume's writer may carry it out.* **This repair did not consolidate, because consolidating in the middle of a batch's own repair is how a state layer loses the row that told it to consolidate.** Payoff target: the Volume 08 close, which is the boundary the rule names, and the archive.

**85. OPEN, ADDED BY THIS REPAIR, AND IT IS ITEMS 63, 70 AND 77 FOR THE FOURTH TIME, AND THIS TIME THE REPAIR ITSELF COULD NOT REPRODUCE THE COLUMN.** **The fifteen-word, thirteen-word and twenty-word maximal-shared-run columns in `state/current.md` do not reproduce under a reading derived from the page rather than quoted from the record: the recorded twenty-word column is 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 2 / 1 / 1 and a derived reading returns twice that in most files, and the recorded fifteen-word column is 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 2 / 1 / 1 while the recorded overlapping-positions column in the same sentence is 20 / 20 / 17 / 18 / 17 / 16 / 17 / 26 / 15 / 19.** The columns were not overwritten and the repair says so in the same sentence that reports it, which is the rule and not an evasion. **What the repair did check, and it is the check that bears on a repair, is the same derived reading run over all five edited chapters before and after the edit, at sixty, forty-nine, forty, thirty-eight, twenty, fifteen and thirteen words: every cell is identical in both arms, so the repair added no lifted run to the closed page and removed none.** The finding is the one item 63 predicts and item 77 has now measured twice: **a check whose reading is quoted into a record instead of derived from the files drifts, and it drifts silently, and the drift is invisible to whoever reads the number next.** Payoff target: the standing check list, where a reading has to be stated as code and not as a sentence, and the volume-close phase, which is the next phase to need these columns.

**And the four debts at Chapter 400, restated by this repair, which discharges none of them.** An owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, four volumes on. Owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's own Batch 0003, which is four and not three. The review gate, which opened and produced findings on all seven Batch 0005 phases the commit history records, and has fallen back to the writing agent every time it has been asked, and has never once produced a review that could be certified independent. And the open items that are not plot, with the sentence bound among them, which are now eighty-five. **The number of debts has not moved in eight volumes and the number of phases that have claimed to discharge one is zero, and this section is a repair record and is not among them.**

## THE CLOSE OF VOLUME 08 AT CHAPTER 0400 — FOUR ITEMS ADDED TO THE LEDGER BY THIS CLOSE, AND NOT ONE OF THEM IS A PLOT THREAD

*Appended downward by the close phase that took `workspace/volume-08/volume-close/PROMPT.md`. **Nothing above this point is rewritten and no status in any item above is changed by this close.** A close adds items and it discharges none. Items 1 to 85 stand exactly as the sections above carry them, with the one exception this close names in its own item 87, which corrects a figure in this close's own brief and not an item above. **The four below are about this project's own instruments, its own records, and its own pipeline, and no one of them is about the story. `outline/volume-09.md` does not exist, and item 90 is not written here because a close may not invent an outline inside a ledger.**

**86. OPEN, ADDED BY THIS CLOSE, AND IT IS ITEM 80 AT THE SCALE OF THE WHOLE VOLUME, AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME THE SPINE ITSELF HAS BEEN READ.** **The sixth named year holds forty-six weeks and not the forty-eight the calendar gives it, so it is two weeks short, and fifty chapters at one week each do not fit in forty-eight.** Measured from the fifty files: Chapter 0351 is the second day of the first week of the first month of that year and Chapter 0400 is the fourth day of the fourth week of the first month of the seventh, and between them the second month is three weeks long — Chapter 0355 is its first week, Chapter 0356 prints its third and Chapter 0357 its fourth — and the third month is three weeks long, because Chapter 0360 prints the third week of the third month and Chapter 0361 opens the fourth month. **Chapter 0379 carries no date line at all, and that is a third defect and a different kind of one: it is the third week of the eighth month, standing between Chapter 0378's second and Chapter 0380's fourth, so the eighth month is an ordinary four-week month and what is missing is the printing of a date and not the week itself. Forty-five files print a date line and the year holds forty-six weeks, and those are two numbers.** The consequence for the year name is that the seventh named year begins at Chapter 0397 where `outline/volume-08.md` predicted Chapter 0399; the name was added and not moved, is written out in full at all four of its occurrences, and appears nowhere in the three hundred and ninety-six chapters before it, so **the rule was applied correctly to a calendar two weeks shorter than the outline's and the rule is not what is wrong.** This close repaired none of it. **A close writes no prose, and a repair that chooses a week is a guess, and items 25, 31 and 83 are three guesses this project has already made and one of them is still standing.** Payoff target: the Volume 09 outline, which is going to plan a calendar for a new volume, and the standing check list, which has no calendar line in it and has never had one. **The blind spot beside the result: not one check in this project can see a date, and a date is the only number in the volume a reader can verify with a pencil, and the repair of Batch 0005 found four intervals wrong in ten weeks and this close found two more in the volume's spine, and every other figure in the same records was right.** **And the arithmetic in this item was wrong twice and is corrected by the repair of this close, and both wrong forms are named here and neither is deleted. It printed *forty-six dated weeks*, which conflated forty-six weeks with the forty-five that print a date, and it printed Chapter 0379 as *the second defect and the smaller one*, which put an undated chapter inside a count of short months and so made the year's shortfall read as three weeks when the two short months are the whole of it. The finding is not smaller than it was; it is a different finding, and the ledger had one entry where it has two kinds of fault in one sentence.**ere `outline/volume-08.md` predicted Chapter 0399; the name was added and not moved, is written out in full at all four of its occurrences, and appears nowhere in the three hundred and ninety-six chapters before it, so **the rule was applied correctly to a calendar two weeks shorter than the outline's and the rule is not what is wrong.** This close repaired none of it. **A close writes no prose, and a repair that chooses a week is a guess, and items 25, 31 and 83 are three guesses this project has already made and one of them is still standing.** Payoff target: the Volume 09 outline, which is going to plan a calendar for a new volume, and the standing check list, which has no calendar line in it and has never had one. **The blind spot beside the result: not one check in this project can see a date, and a date is the only number in the volume a reader can verify with a pencil, and the repair of Batch 0005 found four intervals wrong in ten weeks and this close found two more in the volume's spine, and every other figure in the same records was right.**

**87. ## THE REVIEW REPAIR OF THE VOLUME 08 CLOSE — NO ITEM IS CLOSED, NO THREAD IS RESOLVED, NO DEBT IS DISCHARGED, AND THE LEDGER IS CORRECTED WHERE IT DESCRIBED ITSELF

*Appended downward by the review repair, after the close and after every edit the close made. **`state/open-threads.md` now stands at eighty-nine items, the same number, and holds sixty-three numbered entries, which is four more than it held before this repair and none of the four is an item.** Nothing above this point is rewritten and no status in any item above is changed, with one exception this section names: items 86 and 87 each carried a figure of their own that was wrong, and both are corrected in place with the wrong figure named and not deleted. The full reading is `reviews/volume-08-volume-close.md`.*

**Item 86 is corrected on the arithmetic and not on the finding.** The sixth named year holds forty-six weeks, being Chapters 0351 to 0396 at one week each, against the forty-eight the calendar gives, and is two weeks short. It printed *forty-six dated weeks*, and the forty-sixth is the one that is not dated, and it printed Chapter 0379 as *the second defect and the smaller one*, which put an undated chapter inside a count of short months and made the shortfall read as three. **A chapter is a week whether or not it dates itself**, so 0379 is a third defect of a different kind — the third week of an ordinary four-week eighth month, standing between 0378's second and 0380's fourth, missing its date and not missing from the year. The finding is not smaller than it was and the item is not discharged; it is a different finding, and one ledger entry had been carrying two kinds of fault in one sentence.

**Item 87 is corrected on the in-person figure.** He stood in person in six of the fifty, not four: 0355, 0365, 0371, 0379, 0390 and 0399. The item's own argument is that a count is not an answer, and it published an uncounted number as its closing evidence, which is the failure this project has been naming against itself since Volume 06 and which this item was written to prevent. It is corrected and it is not discharged.

**The ledger's own description of its numbering is corrected, and the number is unchanged at eighty-nine.** This file holds sixty-three numbered entries, being items 1 to 17, 34, 43 to 48 and 51 to 89. Twenty-six numbers are not in it: items 18 to 33 and 35 to 42 were moved into `state/archive/` by the enforcements of the size bound, item 49 is in `state/archive/continuity-volumes-01-to-05.md`, and **item 50 is in no ledger in this project and was never used.** So every *eighty-five items* and *eighty-nine items* in this project's records is the highest number used and not a count of entries, and a reader who looks for forty-nine, or for thirty-one, will not find it here. **Item 43 is a debt and not a figure and it is not repaired.**

**The third debt's history is corrected and the third debt is not discharged.** The gate did not fail to open; it opened and produced findings on all seven Batch 0005 phases the commit history records, and fell back to the writing agent every time it has been asked, and has never once produced a review that could be certified independent. **And the inference the old claim made ran backwards, which is worth naming because it is the same shape of error as the two blockers above: `scripts/novel_runner.sh` writes that fix commit only when the review step exits zero and its log holds a finding, so the commit is proof that the gate opened, and its absence would never have been proof that it did not. A record that reads silence as a failure has stopped reading.** **The debt was never the gate's failure to open, and it is not the gate's independence either, which is a fact about `.opencode/agent/novel-reviewer.md` that is a controller file and is carried as item 43: what is owed is a review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, three second readings, and a reading of this close by a hand that is not the one that wrote it, and none of the three is paid by a correction.**

**No thread in the Volume 09 table above moved.** No thread is closed, merged, resolved or made anybody's; the next asking is nobody's yet; the who is not named; the Lowcross figure is not printed; the question in the second of the eleven books carries no full stop; the two people in their boxes stay in them; and the four separations hold, including the fourth, added by Volume 08's repair. **A handoff is where a lock gets dropped, so this section closes by restating the four debts once more in the corrected form, and a Volume 09 writer who inherits a ledger of eighty-nine items, sixty-three entries, and no debt has been misinformed by somebody.**

# FROM `state/character-state.md`, 2 BLOCK(S), 129073 BYTES, MOVED VERBATIM
## THE VOLUME 08 SECTION, AT CHAPTER 350 — THE SAME PEOPLE, THE NEXT-VOLUME QUESTION ATTACHED, AND WHAT THE FIRST BATCH IS HANDED

*Written by the same close, in the same file, below the section above and rewriting nothing in it. **Volume 08 has no outline of record and this section is not one; it is the state of the people at the moment the volume turns over, and the shape Volume 08 inherits, and the standing instruction that no chapter of its first ten may make a decision this volume left unmade.** The next-volume question, as the closed page gives it: **what it costs the man at the foot of a sheet to be asked once, given that he has said the same true thing twice in nineteen years and been asked nothing, and that about nine of about nine hundred a year are not what the person above him wrote, and that there is no form for a disagreement at the foot of a sheet, and no form for the asking, and that a question in his own building is a record and a record is what the sheet goes in. It is the next asking and it is nobody's yet, and it is not the who and it is not the second half, and the second half of the whole of this is not asked in any document this close wrote.** The answer to the volume that has just closed is no, it took two years, and nobody is going to work it out again in the next one.*

- **The man of about thirty-four with a fair hand is the one Volume 08 must open on and must not send anywhere.** He is findable by a room: four steps and a landing, a table, a chair stood against the wall the bed is against, a window about a foot wide looking at another house's back, about four feet of bare wall with a nail in it and nothing ever hung on it, and a lamp that is not lit until about the seventh hour. **He is the only person in this matter anybody could put a question to and he is the one nobody has asked, and a volume about a question that cannot be asked has to open on a man who does not ask one.** He is owed four things and has collected none of them, and the next asking is his to make and nobody has asked him to make it. **He may not be thanked, may not reach a stage, may not light the lamp early, may not be given a wife who is told where he has gone, and may not be given the decision about anybody's judgment.**

- **The man of about sixty-one is the address and he is owed the asking, and Volume 08 may not ask it.** About nine hundred sheets a year, a second hand since he was forty-two, cannot read a paragraph, about nine sheets a year in nineteen years not as the person above him wrote, about four minutes, three visits to a rail in nineteen years all in a season, and about nine miles each way on a day he is not paid for. **A canon figure that counts visits, turns, non-uses or arrivals is not a figure a chapter may confirm or deny, and the only safe sentence is one that names the figure he has in his head and stops there — that is item 26 and it is now a rule for Volume 08 rather than a note about one chapter.**

- **The two people in their boxes stay in them and the two hundred and forty foot of bank and the sixty children stay where they are.** Nobody asks the form to be void, because no form in this empire has ever been asked to be void and there is no procedure and the person who administers one is a clerk who is not a villain. **The second half of the matter's question is not asked, the who is not named, the guarantee is not printed in its own words, no child is named in it, the Lowcross bill stands at nineteen pounds three and fourpence unpaid with nobody liable, and about nineteen people are still behind the bank.**

- **The two who are about four hundred and thirty miles apart stay about four hundred and thirty miles apart.** Neither knows the other exists, neither has been told the other said it, and no form in this empire would let either be told. **The four hundred and thirty miles is walked zero times in Volume 08 as it was zero times in Volume 07, and the practice stays a practice and does not become a list, a heading, a fund or a company.**

- **The three people who are owed an ordinary week and get one: a foreman of fifty-one who owes a second sentence, a book-keeper of thirty-eight who wants the rest of it and has not asked, and a woman of twenty who is owed three of four women untold.** **In every chapter, the holder of every debt is named by the thing that distinguishes him and not by his trade, and the whole debt table is run holder by holder before a chapter is opened, because a debt given to the wrong man moves a fact about a person and no bound in the standing list can see it.** That is item 21's rule and it is a practice and not a theory.

- **The people who are not spoken to, unthanked and unsent for, and are not going to start: a girl of seventeen, a reader of seventeen, a man of about thirty from the second gang, about four men in a bay who cannot make sense of a paragraph, and a man of about fifty-five with a chain who is not one of them and says so.** **Nobody is thanked anywhere in Volume 08's first ten chapters either, and the not going to be thanked is not anybody's decision, and the two of seventeen are not going to start and the girl is not given the chance to say no.**

- **Marek Kest and Tamsin Rook, unchanged, and the shape of the bond is two instruments and not one and a decision that is unmade.** **No romance begins in Volume 08 and none is implied, and the decision about whether she trusts his judgment may not be made in its first ten chapters; he does not go up a stair because going up it would put her in the room, and nobody is sent and there is no form that would let anybody send him; his reason for not reaching a stage is in about nine words on the page and may not be paid a second time.**

# VOLUME 08, BATCH 0001, CHAPTERS 0351 TO 0360 — THE PEOPLE AFTER THE FIRST TEN WEEKS, EVERY FIGURE MEASURED FROM THE FILES, AND WHAT THE SECOND BATCH IS HANDED

*Appended downward by the run that wrote Chapters 0351 to 0360. **Nothing above this point is rewritten. No person in this section has changed state in a way any chapter of this batch is allowed to record, and the section says so by naming what did not move as carefully as what a reader would want named.** The four debts are restated in `state/open-threads.md` and this phase discharges none of them.*

**What ten weeks did to nobody.** The count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is **seven** and it did not move. Nobody was thanked, nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for, nothing was resolved, and the next asking is nobody's yet. **The three people this state layer keeps apart stayed in three rooms in three chapters: the man of about sixty-one nine miles off a building off the old river road, the woman of about twenty-six at the end of that cold passage nine miles off a lane off the Slade, and the woman of twenty about four hundred and thirty miles up this river in a furnace town. No chapter of the ten put any two of them in one room and no chapter stated or denied a distance between any two of them.**

- **The man of about thirty-four with the fair hand (Chapters 0351 and 0359) is the volume's first page and its household.** He is findable by the room over a chandler's shop: four steps up from the street, a landing, a table, a chair stood over against the wall the bed is against, a window a foot wide or thereabouts, about four feet of bare plaster with one nail in it that was there before he came, and a lamp that is not lit until about the seventh hour. **He has a number in his head that he has had about two years and has never written and never said, and nobody has ever come up those four steps and asked him for it, and the reason he cannot give it away is that the giving of it would be a list, and a list of persons is a company, and a company is a heading.** He is on somebody's list four days a season and is not on one this week and did not put himself on one. He paid fourpence for a copy in each of two other rooms and is in neither. He is owed four things and has collected none: the bill for being findable, which is not his and stands on somebody else's account and whose owner he has not looked up; the thing nobody has thanked him for; a form for asking a person whether they will mind being told a thing before they are told it, which he has looked for about two years; and a woman at the foot of about four steps about four miles off who is not thanked either and has said out loud that she is not going to ask him about his week. **He has had a question ready for about two years, it takes about four seconds to say, he is not going to say it, he is not going to be the eighth, and the next asking is not his to make.** In Chapter 0359 the same man is at the bottom of four steps of his own house with a fire gone out at about the fourth hour: six weeks before he did the last thing of that kind he told her about it without asking her first and gave the reason that if he had asked she would have said no or said yes and been frightened, and this time he is not telling her at all, and the not telling is the same absence as the not asking. **No romance begins, none is implied, and nothing in either chapter decides whether anybody trusts anybody's judgment. The bill for being findable is named twice in his own mouth and the figure nineteen pounds three and fourpence is named zero times in the batch.**

- **Nell Kest, twenty, in a counting room at the back of a dye end about four hundred and thirty miles up this river (Chapter 0352).** Sixteen books, fifteen of them other people's and none with an office on the cover, the sixteenth with her own name at the head of it and no question ever put against it, and a room about eleven feet by fourteen with a bench, a window with a broken light in it, about forty books on shelves built for taller books and a stove behind the bench that whoever gets in first lights and lets go out. **The second week of the third month of the year after is the week her wage stopped arriving, and the man sitting on it cannot say what it is sitting against, and she is not going to ask him, and the decision is hers and was made before this week and did not move this week.** She has told one woman of four that she is not paid and has said the whole of a rule to a second of the four in about four minutes; **the two she cannot reach are the same two she could not reach before she said anything.** A foreman off a dye end has a notice in a coat that says a sum and not what it is against and he has named no day for sending it. The eleven houses off the dyers' lane came in on the first of the two days and eleven numbers went out on the second and the numbers are the same eleven numbers. **Nobody came up those stairs for the sixteenth book this week and nobody has ever sent for it, and she is not going up this river this season, and that is not the same as never and nobody has asked her either thing.** The new fact of the chapter is ordinary and is not a turn: an ordinary day of numbers in and numbers out is what a practice looks like from inside it.

- **Marn Ottery, thirty-four, at the fourth counter of a Table in Auremar (Chapter 0353).** Eleven years at that counter and not a lawyer, at the top of a stair with no other way up, open the second hour to the sixth, about nine people a day coming up in a week when the weather is against them, a fee board of four items and not one of them time. **She is the reason she is still standing at that counter and has never said so out loud to anybody and nobody has thanked her for it.** She told the border law out loud this week, the shape before the thing: a column with a heading over it is a machine and a machine can be asked things all day; a column with no heading over it is a person, and a person is only ever asked one way, out loud in a room by somebody who wants something. A thing put to a border household at no charge has to be answered and the answer entered as a form and not a letter, and it got an answer in about three weeks where a House's own table took about eleven and then said no and cost a hundred and forty years, **and the reason is that the law does not need a person, because a border household is not a person either.** The box of about four hundred bought and unfilled blanks has been under that counter eleven years, no form was ever drawn to account for it, nobody has ever asked her what it is for, and the box is not a post and is not an offer. **The fourth item on the fee board is the one three clerks cannot account for, she wrote it there in her own hand about four years ago and was not in the room when she wrote it or does not think she was, she has said that out loud twice and nobody thanked her either time, and nobody at that counter has put one question to another about it out loud in about four years.** She asked nothing this week and was asked nothing.

- **Tamsin Rook, twenty-four, at the other end of that counter (Chapter 0354), and the fact that she is asked nothing in this batch is entered as a choice and not as a fault.** She has been at that counter about two years. **Nobody has asked her anything in about two years and she has wanted to be asked every working day since about the seventh month of next year**, and the inherited phrase stands once in its own words: *a person who is not asked has not been given a duty*. One man came up that stair once, in the second week of the tenth month, put one question into the air, did not wait for the answer and got none; that is the last time anybody has put a question to her, and she has not decided since whether she wishes she had answered it. **Her undertaking of the fourth of the first month has not been retired, has not been amended and has never been exercised, about two years, and the reason she gives this time is not a variation of the three she gave before: nobody asked her, and there is no form anywhere in this empire for asking a person whether they will mind being told a thing before they are told it, and she has looked in about nine places for one and found a column with a heading over it in every one of them, and a machine is the thing that never asks anybody anything.** The line in her own book about the woman at the other end of the counter is a window and not a wall, and it is a wall the moment she is asked whether she minds its being there. **The four things in the drawer are unopened, the box of blanks is not hers, and the decision about whether she trusts a man's judgment is unmade, was not made in Volume 07, is not made here, and is not going to be made in a dead hour at the end of a counter.**

- **Marek Kest, thirty-eight, in a rented room two streets off a street that goes down to the river road (Chapter 0355), and what this chapter establishes is two refusals and two non-uses and all four are paid for.** He does not attempt the reading, and the reason is his own and he gave it out loud in the room, to himself and to about four feet of wall: it has been done to him twice, each time it took a whole day out of his mother's own history, he was not in the room for that day and was watching her at a table in a house in a lane in this town, he has the address of it and the length of it, and he is thirty-eight and cannot tell whether he came out of that house or stood in the doorway of it and waited. **The third one is not a thing he is going to spend on a man at a rail.** He does not do the other thing either, the one he is able to do, which would take about four minutes and a clean sheet and has been done once and worked; **a correct non-use is entered as one, and the reason is nine words long and it is on a page in another year and this volume is not going to say it a second time.** He is not at a stage and is not going to arrive at one, and nothing in this empire would put a man in a rented room and examine him about one. **The mark is about three inches long on the inside of his left wrist and it reads nothing and it decides nothing and it does not know where the ford is; the wage is the price and the mark is what he has instead of a receipt, and there is no form anywhere that gives a man a receipt for what he has paid.** A bar of his own came off him on the twelfth of the ninth month of last year on an application of one line which he wrote himself. He holds no paper, no office, no fund and no list, and he chose a labour a man is free to refuse out of his own hand and it is not being re-decided. **A woman about four miles off is not told that he said the reason out loud to about nine men in a bay, and nobody is going to tell her.**

- **Halla Wray, fifty-one, foreman, in a box nobody has asked her about (Chapter 0356).** The bay at the top of a lane off the Slade is about nine foot by eleven with a bench longer than the bay is wide and a lamp that is not lit until about the seventh hour. The job came to her because nine families agreed between themselves in a shed behind the second bay, about nine years ago, in about an hour and a half. **She has been inside that box since the first week of the seventh month of the year after, entered by a class, and nobody has asked her about it and nobody is going to, and being in a box and not being asked about it are the same arrangement and the arrangement was made in that shed by nine families and not by her, and she has had about two years to find a way of saying it as though it were two things and there is not going to be one.** The entries were about a class; the class is a heading; a heading is how a person turns into a company; a company is a thing this empire can hold wages against and hand letters to and take to law. She said the rest of it once, in a room with about nine people in it, in the closed part of this: the form did not ask her whether she would represent anybody, it asked who she represents, there is a column for the answer and no column for a refusal, and a person who writes nothing in it has not refused and has not agreed and is still in the class. **She is not going to say it again and nobody thanked her for saying it once.** At the back of that bay the two of seventeen are unspoken to, unthanked, unsent for and not going to start, and there is no answer in that bay about why and not one of the men in it has one, and no paper anywhere asks a person of seventeen whether she would rather not, and the girl has never been given the chance to say no. **About four men in that bay cannot make sense of a paragraph and not one question about a number has ever been put to any of them.** The man of about fifty-five with the chain is not one of the four and says so out loud about once every few months to nobody in particular, and he has known for about four years that findability does not work on him, has never used it, and has never told anybody. **The man who keeps the books of that Company is four hundred yards up the same lane and has wanted the second half of a sentence of hers since the third month of the year after, and he has not asked and is not going to, and the day he asks is the day she stops saying the half she has got.** The cross in the chalk is at the height of the third link of that chain, it means nothing, it is nobody's, it is about four years off being anybody's evidence, and nothing in that lane is going to turn either one of them into anything.

- **The man of about sixty-one, the address, who signs at the foot of every sheet (Chapter 0357).** The second hand since he was forty-two, in a room on the ground floor of a building off the old river road with a shelf of eleven books, a table, a chair, and a window about two feet wide that has been without a pane for about four years. **He cannot read a paragraph: he can read a figure, a name and most of the short words that hold a sheet together, and everything else is shape, and he has been working out which is which for nineteen years.** He reads what he can and signs the rest, about nine hundred times a year, and he signs for people who cannot write and for people who are not in the room. **About nine of those sheets a year are not what the person above him wrote, in about nineteen years, and he signed every one of them because he was not asked and was given nothing to sign against, and he is the only person alive who knows which ones they were and he cannot write one of them down and has never told anybody.** There is no form for believing a thing you cannot read and there is nowhere in this empire for a man to put his mark beside a sum and call it a disagreement, and it is not an oversight in the making of the page. **What he wants is about four minutes: no to about half of the ones of a year he can remember and yes to the rest, and the yeses matter as much, and there is nobody to ask.** On every one of those sheets there are three things that can be asked and two of them are a clerk and an office and neither of those is a person anybody can put a question to, and that leaves him, and he is the one nobody has asked anything in about nineteen years. **He is not in a bay and he is not one of the four men who cannot make sense of a paragraph; he is nine miles off in a different building, and a list with him in it alongside those four would be a list of five, and there is no such list and there is not going to be one.** He has a figure in his head about how many times he has come to that room in nineteen years and he has never written it down and is not going to, and the distance is about nine miles and he knows it to about a yard. Nobody has ever thanked him, he has never asked to be, and **he knows both halves of that and has never put them in one sentence.** A carrier is paid ninepence at the lock, is on a list, and in about six years nobody has asked her what she carries, and he has not asked her either.

- **The woman of about twenty-six at the end of the cold passage (Chapter 0358), and she is not in this matter and never has been.** She has been in that building about four years, is not one of the three clerks, and stands at a lock at one end of about four hundred yards of cold flags with a room and a rail about four feet off the floor at the far end that is not on the passage's work. **She keeps the count in her head and has never once put a hand on a rack, and her figure has gone up since the seventh month of the year after and she has not said by how much and is not going to; she worked that out in about four minutes standing at a lock, one time, and the figure is the only one this empire keeps on how many people are sitting inside a thing nobody can be asked about, and hers is a count of persons under nothing.** A number in a book is a column and a column wants a heading and a heading over that figure would be a heading over about nine hundred rooms at once, and a person cannot be under a heading like that and stay a person, and she is not going to be the one who puts it there. **Nothing that comes down those flags goes into that room and nothing that comes out of it comes up them, there is no list that reaches it and no rack that stops at it and no person whose work takes her to that door, and that is the whole of the reason a thing said out loud in that room stays in that room, and it is not because anybody is careful.** She has three questions for the man of about sixty-one and has asked none of them, and the fact that he came at all gave her no fourth. She is the only other person in this matter who knows what was said in that room, she was not in the room and has never been in it, and there is no form anywhere that would let anybody put to her the question of what she heard.

- **The clerk of about fifty-five (Chapter 0360), nineteen years in one room, who writes the minutes, advises nobody and asks nothing.** The room is the one off the old river road with the shelf of eleven books under the window with the pane out of it, about four thousand entries and not one name in any of them. **The arrangement that has kept about nine hundred sheets a year out of every book in this empire for nineteen years is the arrangement that has kept anybody from asking the man at the foot of them one question, and those are one fact and not two; she got to that in about four minutes spread over about six years, with a pen on the table and nobody to say it to, and it is not a discovery anybody could act on.** A question put in that building is a question put into a record and the record is what the sheet goes in, and the last time a thing of that shape happened to about four hundred people their sheet went back to them and nobody was punished. **A minute with an answer in it is an order, an instruction has to be carried out by somebody, the somebody would have a name on it and a name on it is a party, so she has not entered one in nineteen years, and not entering one is the price of the only thing anybody has ever asked her to do, which is to be right.** Her own finding of about six years of her own motion is on the shelf under the window with the day and the hour on it and her own name at the foot, and it is the only thing on that shelf with a name at the foot, and it is still a question, has not been turned into a sentence and has no full stop on it. **She is the only person in this matter anybody could serve a question on in writing and she is paid nothing for standing ready to have it served.** A quarter of it was answered out loud in a room at the far end of her own passage in the second month by a man who walked nine miles to get there on his own two feet, and she entered none of that either, and nobody is going to ask her about it a second time, and there is no form anywhere in this empire for a person to ask themselves whether they would still have written it. **She is not told anything about what was said in that room and she wrote none of it down and nobody is going to ask her why she wrote none of it, and there is nothing in this empire that would let a clerk of an office be told that a person is coming or that a person is not, and that is the price of the standing ready and nobody laid it out and she has never had the chance to find out whether she would do it again.**

- **The people who are not spoken to and not thanked, and none of them moved.** The girl of seventeen and the reader of seventeen at the back of the bay; **the reader is paid forty-five pence a day and four days a week and a day of that is anywhere between four pence and a shilling according to what gets set on the bench on the day.** About four men in that bay who cannot make sense of a paragraph. The man of about fifty-five with a chain, who is not one of them and never is, and who used the one thing he has had for about four years once, at a shut gate, and told nobody. A woman of about forty with a scuttle, who is not in this batch. A woman of about thirty-five next door, who came out on her own step twice and said nothing and was spoken to by nobody. A foreman off a dye end with a notice in a coat that is not sent. A carrier paid ninepence at the lock who is on a list. A man of about forty who came up four steps for a copy and does not know the name of the man who copied it. About nineteen people behind a bank, and about sixty children inside a guarantee that is not printed in its own words.

- **What the second batch is handed.** The shape and not the instrument: a practice in two people about four hundred and thirty miles apart with no list, no form, no heading and no fund; about nine hundred entries a year still coming in under a heading with a second hand at the foot of every one of them; about nine of the nine hundred a year not as the person above him wrote; and nobody having worked out how a thing is asked in about nine hundred buildings at once by people who cannot be instructed to ask it, which is the answer the last volume gave in the negative, in a room, in about nine minutes, and it took two years and it is worth a room. **The next asking is nobody's yet. The second half of this matter's question is not asked and is not restated in any form a writer could put in a mouth. Nobody in the next ten chapters may ask anybody anything out loud, and nothing may be thanked, forgiven, sent for or resolved, and the two people in their boxes stay in them.**

## THE CAST AT CHAPTER 360, AFTER THE REPAIR OF BATCH 0001, AND IT IS ONE ROSTER AND NOT TWO

*Written by the repair phase that took `logs/batch-0001.review.log`. **This is a repair record and not a review record.** The Volume 07 handoff section above is the roster as it stood at Chapter 350 and it is superseded by this one for the purposes of a Volume 08 writer; the Volume 07 close sections are in `state/archive/rolling-volume-07-records.md`. **No state of any person changed in the repair. What changed is that ten of them were on the page contradicting somebody, which is the first time in this volume that most of them had been in a scene at all.**

**Marek Kest, thirty-eight, the protagonist.** On the page in one chapter of this batch, 0355, and in his own room, and that is one chapter against a floor of three a movement now written into `outline/volume-08.md`, and the floor is not met by this batch and is named as not met. **He refused the reading and did not exercise the thing he is able to, he is not at a stage, he is on no list and in no office, and the mark on the inside of his left wrist is about three inches long and reads nothing.** He went to a gate twice this week and was stood at it once. He was not thanked, he was not sent for, and nobody asked him anything.

**The man of about thirty-four with the fair hand.** Said no to a client who had a right to be told his own fifth line was wrong, out loud, to his face, and copied the line as written, and went on copying it. The change is that the refusal now has a person on the other end of it. His number is still in his head, unwritten and unsaid, and the next asking is still not his to make.

**The woman of twenty, Nell Kest.** Told a second of the four women the whole of a rule out loud in a room, and was told by that woman that it had gone nowhere, that two of the woman's own four had stopped, and that she is not going to be the one who says it to a third one. **The wage is still held, nineteen days of a crew's money in a coat four miles off, and she is still not going to ask what it is held against.** Her sixteenth book is still not asked for.

**Marn Ottery, thirty-four.** Said the whole of a rule out loud once more in this batch, in a chapter that is otherwise unchanged, and was not thanked. **She wrote the fourth item on that counter's fee board herself, about four years ago, and has now said so out loud to the woman four feet away from her, and the woman four feet away did not look up while she said it and has not stopped being the person who is not asked.**

**Tamsin Rook, twenty-four.** Said out loud to a fee board that she has wanted to object for about two years and there is nowhere to put the wanting, and **was heard by the woman at the other end of the same counter, and the hearing is the change.** Neither of them thanked the other. The undertaking is unretired, unamended and unexercised, and the fourth of the four things in the drawer is where it has been and no key to that drawer has ever been offered to her.

**Halla Wray, fifty-one.** Gave a book-keeper half a sentence in a doorway and told him to keep his own half of the exchange on his own side of it. **Being in a box and not being asked about it are the same arrangement, and the arrangement was made in a shed by nine families and not by her, and that is unchanged.** The two of seventeen are still at the back of the bay and the chalk cross still means nothing.

**The man of about sixty-one.** Told a carrier, out loud, that the fifth of the four sheets in front of him is the one he would not read out loud, and was told by the carrier that she is paid ninepence at the lock and is not paid to remember things. **He signed all four and about nine hundred more in the year and about nine of the year are still not what the person above him wrote, and he is still the only person alive who knows which ones they were.** Nobody asked him anything, and a clerk of about fifty-five put four sheets on her table at the fourth hour and he told her the flags were cold, and he has now been in a room with her on the page, which he has not been in a volume, and neither of them said the other thing.

**The clerk of about fifty-five.** Entered four sheets in a hand that does not vary and let a man stand in her doorway while the light went off the wall above the shelf. **Her own finding is still a question on the shelf with no full stop on it and her own name at the foot, and nobody has served a question on her in writing, and she is paid nothing for standing ready to have it served.**

**The woman of about twenty-six.** Told a rack-man that one is the usual number, and he told her that a lock is not a person. **Her figure is still in her head, has still gone up, and is not being handed on.**

**The man of about fifty-five with the chain.** Untouched by the repair, which is named rather than hidden: he is in 0356 and the chapter is about the bay, and the one thing he has and does not use again is card 0364's, in the next batch. **He is not one of the four who cannot make sense of a paragraph and says so, and the chain is not a symbol.**

**The people with no scene in this batch, and that is a fact about the batch and not about them: the two of seventeen, the four men who cannot make sense of a paragraph, the about nine families who met in a shed, the three clerks at the fourth counter, the landlord nine miles off, and the wife at the foot of four steps, who is in 0359 and who is the only person in the batch who is thanked by anybody, which is by nobody.**

# CHARACTER STATE AT CHAPTER 370, AFTER THE SECOND TEN WEEKS OF VOLUME 08

*Written by the phase that took `workspace/volume-08/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`. Nothing above this point is rewritten. It is a writing record and it is not a review, and it discharges none of the four debts. Every entry below says what the person wanted on the page, what the ten weeks cost them, and what they did not do.*

**Nobody in this batch was asked anything, nobody was thanked, nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for, and nothing was resolved.** The count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is seven at Chapter 350, seven at Chapter 360 and seven at Chapter 370, and the next asking is nobody's yet.

**Marek Kest, thirty-eight, the protagonist.** In one chapter of the ten, 0365, on the stair of a counter about four miles off, carrying a box of about four hundred blanks up four steps because a clerk asked him for it with her face and neither of them had anything else to do with their hands. He said four or five sentences. The one that mattered was to Marn Ottery: a thing four people have seen is a thing that can be entered, and the entering is a list, and the list has a clerk's name at the foot of it, and that is the whole of the danger to a woman who has kept paper nobody asked about for eleven years. He also said, out loud, that the name he is after is not on the sheet he came to have copied and was not on it the last time anybody looked either. He did not attempt the reading, did not exercise the thing he is able to do, is not at a stage, holds no office, fund, list or leave, and has a mark about three inches long on the inside of his left wrist that reads nothing and decides nothing. **He went down four flights without his copy, was paid nothing, was not served and was not asked for anything, and the count did not move while he was on the stair.** The volume outline sets a floor of three chapters a movement for him and this batch meets it at one; that is a shortfall, it is named in the batch record, and it is carried into the next movement's cards as a debt.

**The man of about thirty-four with the fair hand.** In two of the ten, 0361 and 0369, and the same man in both. In 0361 he stands at a rail for a week in bad weather and buys nothing, and Marn Ottery tells him that about four people instead of about nine are coming up that stair this week and that the ones who are not coming are the ones who have furthest to come, and that the rail is where they decide. He hears it and does nothing with it, because there is no form for sending for a person and she will not start with him. In 0369 he comes home to a house four miles off and stands on a step while his wife says out loud that not asking is not the same as not minding, and that he has been waiting two years for her to ask and that it is not going to happen. He has a figure in his head he has never written down and has never given to anybody, and he did not give it to her. **He is on nobody's list, he buys nothing at a counter, he is not thanked, he is not asked anything, and the next asking is not his to make.**

**Halla Wray, fifty-one, a foreman in a box entered by a class.** In 0367, with the gate at the bottom of the lane open, which nobody in that lane decided. She said out loud that a foreman who is findable is a foreman who can be served, and a man of twenty-eight told her that a shut gate is doing the work of nine men for nothing and that he would rather they had nine men. She is not flattered and does not tell him to stop. The gate was shut again on the second day. The class is still a heading, the form still asks who she represents and has no column for a refusal, the owed second half of a sentence did not arrive, and the two of seventeen at the back of that bay were not spoken to and the girl was not offered a way of saying no. **She was not asked anything about the box and has not been for two years, and the cost of that is the same fact as the protection, and the difference between them is a man of twenty-eight who works out the arithmetic in seven years and says it in a bay.**

**Marn Ottery, thirty-four, at the fourth counter, eleven years, not a lawyer.** In two of the ten, 0361 and 0365. In 0361 she refuses to say a thing out loud a third time and says why: a thing said twice to the same man is a thing with a shape, and a person who has said a thing twice is a person who says things. In 0365 about four hundred bought and unfilled blanks are carried up four steps into the received room and set down and not opened, and she says out loud that there is no form anywhere with a line on it for four people seeing a thing happen, and the box is the nearest thing this empire has to a place a person could put a question nobody has ever been asked, and it is still not a place after it has been carried. **She was not thanked by anybody, she is not on a list, the box is on the floor of the received room with the lid where it was, and no form records the stair.**

**Nell Kest, twenty, about four hundred and thirty miles up this river.** In 0366, in the counting room at the back of a dye end with the stove out. The second of the four women she told a rule came back and asked, in effect, for it to be said again, and Nell refused, and said the reason out loud: a rule said twice to the same person is a person somebody can be served with. The second woman refused that, and the rule was said a second time in about four minutes, and it is now in two heads in one lane instead of one and it went nowhere again. **She is not going to ask the man who holds her wage what it is held against, and that decision is hers and was made before this batch and did not move. Nineteen days of a crew's money is in a coat four miles off. She is not going up this river this season. Nobody came up those stairs for the sixteenth book and nobody ever has.**

**The man of about sixty-one, the second hand since he was forty-two.** In 0368, in the ground-floor room off the old river road, with about nine hundred sheets a year and a pane still out of the window. There was a sheet in front of him that was not what the man above him wrote and he signed it because he was not asked. A young man of twenty-six came with a basket and initialled the corner of each sheet and has never read one, and he was told out loud, to a room with a window in it, that that one was wrong, and said he is paid for a corner and not for a page, and that talking to the only person in the room is not the same as talking to the person, and that the sheet goes down the corridor and does not come back up. **He said one true sentence out loud to a person who could do nothing with it, which is the first time in this batch that anybody said a true thing to another person and was asked nothing back. He is owed the asking and about four minutes and there was nobody to ask. His own figure is not confirmed and not added to and not dated. He was not thanked.**

**The clerk of about fifty-five, nineteen years in that room.** In 0370, in the same room, with the pane still out of it. A minute with an answer in it is an order and in nineteen years she has entered none and no form lets her. A woman of nineteen at the far end of the room, six weeks in the job, said that a question with no full stop on it is not a question but a note, and a note can be thrown out, and she is not going to be the one who keeps it. The clerk told her she was right and said the difference between them is that she has never once swept it off that shelf, and that there is no form anywhere that will let her put a person's name against that. **The young woman did not throw it out and does not know why, and neither clerk in the room is going to say out loud that she has not. Six years of the clerk's own motion are on that shelf and are still a question with no full stop on it and her own name at the foot, and a quarter of it was answered out loud in a room at the cold end of her own passage in the second month by a man who came nine miles on foot, and she entered none of it and is not going to be asked about it a second time. She is the only person in this matter anybody could serve a question on in writing and nobody has ever paid her for standing ready.**

**The man of about fifty-five with a chain.** In 0364, in the bay, in a week in which he does not use the one thing he has. Two slates are on the bench and one of them is face up with a question in an office hand and no name under it, and he cannot read a paragraph and so cannot read it, and the one thing he can do would not tell him what is on it either. A man of twenty-eight told him that out loud, which is the first time in seven years anybody has said it to him. He said the shape of it out loud in four sentences to about nine men and nothing happened. **He is not one of the four who cannot read a paragraph and says so out loud to nobody, he has known for about four years that the one thing he can do will not reach him, he has never used it, he used it once at a shut gate and told nobody, and he is not going to be asked one question about a number.**

**A woman of about forty with a scuttle.** In 0362, in the room at the far end of four hundred yards of cold flags, lighting a lamp twice a week. About four people stood in that room before a man of thirty-four did, and he talked for nine minutes, and she said nothing at the rail then and has said nothing since. A clerk of thirty told her he would have to write it down or refuse to and that there is no line on that paper for either, and sent her to the other day of the week. She told him that is not the rota. Then she said the true thing, which is about herself: she has got so good at not hearing that she would not know now whether anything had been said in there at all. **There is not one form anywhere in this empire that will let anybody ask her what was said in that room, and nobody has asked her and nobody is going to, and that is the same fact as her not having been able to answer if anybody did, and the two are not two things.**

**A woman of about thirty-five with a day-book, and a woman of about thirty-five next door, and a young man of about twenty-six, a lodger of about twenty-two, a woman of about nineteen, a man of about twenty-eight, a man of about thirty from the second gang.** All unnamed, all carried by the thing that distinguishes them, and none of them added to the page as a name. The day-book keeper came about nine miles, was not let in, and has written none of it down, and the not writing is also the thing that protects four people who rent rooms by the week, and she is the only person in this batch who is findable and holding a fact about a man who is not. **The count of consecutive batches that have added no new named figure stands at twenty-two and this batch adds none, which makes it twenty-three.**

**The people who are not in these ten chapters and are not moved: a woman of about twenty-six at the end of that cold passage, whose figure has gone up and is not handed on; a girl of seventeen and a reader of seventeen at the back of a bay, unspoken to, unthanked, unsent for and not going to start; a man of about thirty from the second gang; about nineteen people behind a bank; about sixty children inside a guarantee that is not printed in its own words; Tamsin Rook, twenty-four, with a live and unexercised undertaking and a decision unmade; Rennick Adley, thirty-eight, who wants the rest of a sentence and has not asked.** The two documents stay two documents four hundred and thirty miles apart with no instrument joining them, and only Marek Kest and Tamsin Rook know both exist and neither of them says so.

# WHAT THE REPAIR OF VOLUME 08 BATCH 0002 CHANGED ABOUT THESE PEOPLE, AND WHAT IT DELIBERATELY DID NOT CHANGE

*Appended downward by the phase that read `logs/batch-0002.review.log`. **A repair is not a review. Nothing above this point is rewritten: the roster at the close of Volume 05, what Volume 07 did to it, and the two blocks for Volume 08 all stand as the runs that wrote them wrote them, and the one cast block at Chapter 370 above is the writing phase's own and it is not corrected here.** What follows is only what changed on the page, person by person, and the two entries above that are wrong about one thing each.*

**The man of about fifty-five with the chain is the one entry that was wrong.** The cast at Chapter 370 above says he has known for about four years that the one thing he can do will not reach him, and used it once at a shut gate and told nobody, and those two statements cannot both be true inside the same four years. **The repaired page says it once: the thing reached him about four years ago at a shut gate at the end of a lane, it has not reached him since, he has not turned it over to look at it, and nobody in that bay knows that he knows.** He is still not one of the four who cannot make sense of a paragraph, still says so out loud to nobody in particular about once every few months, still has not been asked one question about a number, still is not going to be asked one, and the chain is still not a symbol. **What is new about him is not a want and not a want refused: it is that the week is now on the page as a gesture that comes to nothing, twice, and the wanting is unchanged, which is the volume's subject and the reason the chapter carries him at all.**

**Marn Ottery, thirty-four, at the fourth counter.** The debt table of this batch's own card said the box of about four hundred blanks was in her mouth in Chapter 0361, and the page did not have it there. **It does now: on the second day she tells a man at her rail what is under the counter without being asked, in the flat voice she keeps for stock — about four hundred bought and unfilled blanks, eleven years, none of them ever filled in, no word on the lid, and she is not starting it with a man who stands at a rail and buys nothing.** She is not thanked, she is not asked one thing back, the box is still not a form for anything, and the fourth item on that board is still unopened. In Chapter 0365 four people saw the box go up four steps and three of them are now on the page, which does not make the four any more enterable.

**The clerk of about fifty-five, nineteen years in that room.** Her own finding is still on the shelf under the window with no full stop on it and her own name at the foot, and she is still the only person in this matter anybody could serve a question on in writing and is still paid nothing for standing ready. **What the repair changed is her last page: the quarter of her finding answered out loud in the second month is now costed rather than restated, because there is no column in any of the eleven books for a quarter of an answer and a quarter is not a thing a minute can be about.** The woman of nineteen at the far end of the room still did not throw it out, and the clerk of fifty-five is still not going to say out loud that the woman of nineteen has not thrown it out, and nobody is asking the clerk a second time.

**The man of about thirty-four with the fair hand.** Unchanged. He is still the only person anybody could put a question to and the one nobody has, still has a number in his head he has never written down or given to anybody, and the repair did not go near his two chapters, because nothing in them was found wrong. His wife at the foot of four steps and the woman next door at four feet are also unchanged, and the woman of about forty with a scuttle is also unchanged.

**Nell Kest, twenty.** Unchanged and at the same decision she made before this batch. Her wage is nineteen days of a crew's money in a coat four miles off, she is not going to ask what it is held against, she is not going up this river this season, and nobody has come up those stairs for the sixteenth book. The rule went into a second head this batch and went nowhere again, and the chapter now says out loud that there is no form for saying a rule again, which is the same fact stated in the room where it was said twice.

**Halla Wray, fifty-one.** Unchanged. The gate was open for an afternoon and shut again, a man of twenty-eight told her a shut gate does the work of nine men for nothing, she was not flattered and did not tell him to stop, the class is still a heading, and the second half of the sentence she owes is still owed.

**The man of about sixty-one.** Unchanged. One true sentence said out loud to a young man paid for a corner, asked nothing back, the figure he carries not confirmed and not added to and not dated, and the pane still out of the window.

**Marek Kest, thirty-eight, the protagonist.** Unchanged in what he wants and in what he holds, which is nothing: no office, no fund, no list, no leave, no stage, a mark three inches long on the inside of his left wrist that reads nothing and decides nothing, and the erased name of his mother and the disputed family claim, which he has not got. **He is on the page once in ten chapters and that is a decision defended in `state/current.md` and not a change made here; the repair added no Marek Kest to any chapter and removed none.** Nobody was thanked, nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for, and nothing was resolved.

# THE CHARACTER STATE AFTER VOLUME 08 BATCH 0003, CHAPTERS 0371 TO 0380

*Appended downward by the run that took `workspace/volume-08/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`. **Nothing above this point is rewritten. This is what these ten weeks did to the people, and it is almost nothing, and the almost nothing is the finding.** Nobody was thanked, nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for, and nothing was resolved, and the count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is seven and it did not move.*

## Who changed, and who did not

**Marek Kest, thirty-eight, the protagonist.** He is on the page in three of these ten chapters, which is the floor `outline/volume-08.md` sets and which the two batches before this one did not meet, and the three are different kinds of presence. In **0371** he is in person at the end of a yard and is not stood at the bar, and the man at the gate says the two figures out loud and the man who decided between them is not there, and Marek says nothing and is not asked nothing and goes home with the page still in his coat. In **0377** he is an absence with a named cost: a man of thirty-one tells a man of sixty-one, on a doorstep nine miles off, that a man of thirty-eight came that whole distance on his own two feet in a month that is gone and that nobody at the far end was told he was on the road. In **0379** he is in person again and pays fourpence for a copy at the top of four flights, and the name he is after is not on it and was not on the last one either. **His want is on the page in his own voice twice, in 0371 and 0379: a line struck out of a page in a hand that is not his mother's, and the name that ought to stand at the end of it.** He still holds nothing: no office, no fund, no list, no leave, no stage. He did not attempt the reading and did not exercise the thing he is able to do. The mark about three inches long on the inside of his left wrist reads nothing and decides nothing. The woman about four miles off at the top of a stair is still not told that he said a reason out loud to about nine men in a bay, and this batch does not tell her.

**Nell Kest, twenty, a dye-house worker about four hundred and thirty miles up this river.** In **0372**. Sixteen books, fifteen on loan, the sixteenth with her own name in capitals at the head of it. **She refused the second of the four women the third telling of a rule she has already given twice, and the reason she gave is about the other woman: a rule that travels once belongs to whoever said it, and a rule that travels twice belongs to whoever hears it, and she has just said out loud that the second woman is somebody a rule could be fetched out of, and she cannot take the telling back.** The second woman had already said the rule to a woman in a fourth house off the lane, all of it, and that woman has stopped speaking to her, so the cost of the second telling fell outside the room before the third was refused. Her wage has not come since the second week of the third month of the year after. She put a twist of kindling from her own bag into a firebox before she left and neither of them said a word about it. She is not going up this river this season, and that is not never, and she has not used the word.

**Tamsin Rook, twenty-four, at the fourth counter.** In **0374**, for the first time in three batches, and the batch in which the winter is what it costs her. **She went down all four flights of a stair to tell a person at the bottom that the counter was shut, which is telling and not asking, and it is the first thing she has done at that counter in two years that cost her anything.** Her undertaking of the fourth of the first month is live and unretired and unexercised and was not exercised today. The four things in the drawer under the board are still shut and Marn Ottery told her out loud, after two years of not saying anything about it, that the drawer is not hers and has not been locked in eleven years and that one of the four things has been hers since before she came, and that she has watched her not open it for two years. Nobody has asked her anything in about two years and nobody asked her anything this week, and she wanted to be asked, and wanting is not owing and owing is not asking, and there is no form for the wanting. She is not on anybody's list. She is not thanked.

**Marn Ottery, thirty-four, at the other end of the same counter, eleven years, not a lawyer.** In **0374** and **0379**. **In 0374 she resists a colleague going down a stair, and then says the thing about the drawer that she has held for two years.** In 0379 she tells a man of thirty-eight, at the third hour of a wet morning, that the fourth thing on the board has been there longer than she has and that she does not know what it is for, and that the name he is after was not on the last copy either, which is the first time she has said that out loud in that room. She has said the shape of a thing out loud in that room twice and been asked one thing back both times, and she is not going to say it a third time. The box of about four hundred bought and unfilled blanks is under that counter and is not opened in either chapter. Nobody thanked her in either chapter.

**Halla Wray, fifty-one, a foreman in a shed at the Slade end.** In **0375**. **The written engagement of the two of seventeen at the back came to the end of its own terms in the week and nobody spoke to either of them, and she was stopped from saying so out loud by a man of about twenty-eight on the grounds that a foreman who explains a paper has put two people in a room with her.** She is still in a box entered by a class and nobody has asked her about it. The second half of a sentence she owes the man who keeps the books four hundred yards up the lane did not arrive. The mark in the chalk on the end wall is still at the height of the third link of a chain and means nothing and is nobody's, and she did not scrape it. She used the word *shed* for that building this week and the batches before used *bay*, and the page uses one name for it and not two.

**The man of about thirty-four with the fair hand.** In **0378**, and referred to in 0379 as a man who comes up a stair and is not in it. **He stood at a bar of iron at the end of a yard six days this week, three shillings a day on a wall that is not on a district's list, and the man who set that figure is not stood at the bar.** He is still the only person in this matter that a question could be put to and not one has been. A number lives in his head and was not written down and was not given to either woman on that lane, and neither woman said the thing that would make it an understanding. A bill came to his door with his name at the head of it and is on somebody else's account, and it lay in the hall and he did not pick it up, and the figure on it was not read out in that lane.
