The woman of about thirty-five next door has been next door eleven years and said out loud that being findable is a bill and is the only part of it anybody on that lane has ever had to hold.

**The man of about sixty-one, the second hand since he was forty-two.** In **0377**. **He did not walk his nine miles in the cold week, and the reason he gave the other man was the road and not the cold, and the reason he gave himself was that nobody at the far end of those flags knew he was on it.** He heard third-hand, from a man of about thirty-one who had it from a boy who does not know what he saw, that a man of thirty-eight came that distance once and was met by nobody. He still wants about four minutes to go through the ones he can remember and say no to about half and yes to the rest, and there is still nobody to give them to. **The figure he carries is neither confirmed nor denied by any chapter of this batch and is not added to.** He is not one of the four who cannot make sense of a paragraph and never has been, and this batch does not put him with them.

**The clerk of about fifty-five, nineteen years in one room off the old river road.** In **0376**. **A woman of nineteen, eight weeks in the job, told her out loud that her name is the only name at the foot of anything in that building and that a person whose name is the only one is a person a question can be put to, and the clerk said the other half of it: a room in which one person signs everything and nobody signs her is a room in which nothing can be asked of anybody, because there is nobody in it to ask.** The change in her: she has begun going out of the room before the last of the four sheets comes in — three times that day, twice the day before — and she said out loud that she has been going out to find out whether a person who is not asked has not been given a duty, and that she does not know what she is protecting the room from. Four sheets went into the eleven books before the fourth hour. Her own note is on the shelf under the window with a day and an hour on it and no full stop, and nobody asked her a second time and the woman of nineteen has not thrown it out.

**The woman of about twenty-six at the end of about four hundred yards of cold flags.** In **0380**, for the first time in three batches, and nobody brought her in and nobody told her anything. **Her count of persons under nothing has gone up again and it is not printed in any form, not written down, and not handed to anybody.** She said out loud, once, to the passage, that the figure has gone up, that she is not telling the man in the felt apron by how much, and that she has three questions for a man of about sixty-one at the other end of those flags and has asked him none of them. The man in the felt apron resisted, saying that a person with a figure about them is a person somebody can hand a thing to, and then said that if the somebody is a man he has been talking to a lock for four years. She has never been in the room at the far end of that passage and has not gone up in four years. **The woman of about forty with a scuttle is in none of these ten files, and the two of them are not in one room anywhere in this volume.**

**The woman of about thirty-five with a day-book, the woman of about twenty-two, the woman at the foot of the four steps, the man at the gate, the man in the felt apron, the man of about thirty-one at the step nine miles off.** In **0373, 0374, 0378, 0371, 0380 and 0377** respectively, and each is carried by what distinguishes him or her and not by a trade. **The woman of twenty-two said out loud, once, in a building with one other person in it, the coming that was not lodged, and the not-writing stopped being one woman's on that afternoon.** The man at the gate has never once argued with anybody in his life and said both figures out loud to three men in a morning. The man of about thirty-one has walked the same nine miles for six years and been met at the other end of it by nobody.

## The named figures, and the count

**This batch added no new named figure, which makes the count of consecutive batches that have added none twenty-four.** The people who appear by name are Marek Kest, Nell Kest, Tamsin Rook, Marn Ottery and Halla Wray, all of them already on the page, and the other two of the seven named people in this matter are absent from these ten weeks. Every capitalised token in the ten files was enumerated and checked against the whole manuscript and one of them is new to the manuscript nowhere.

## The four debts, restated, and this phase discharged none of them

**An owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, four volumes on. Owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's own Batch 0003, which is four and not three. The review gate, which opened and produced findings on all seven Batch 0005 phases the commit history records, and has fallen back to the writing agent every time it has been asked, and has never once produced a review that could be certified independent, and a gate that only ever falls back is not a gate that passed either. And the open items that are not plot.** The number of debts has not moved in eight volumes and the number of phases that have claimed to discharge one is zero, and this record is not among them.

## What an independent reading of these ten chapters found about the people in them

*An independent reading and not a review; it discharges none of the four debts. It confirmed the three people this state layer keeps apart stayed apart and that no chapter of this batch puts any two of them in one room, and it confirmed that the four who cannot make sense of a paragraph are not asked one question about a number. It found three things in Chapter 0379 that would have made the man of thirty-eight a person who had asked a clerk something and been answered, which would have made him a party, and the two of them were the clerk's own words at that counter that he was not. **All three are gone and the man of thirty-eight is not a person who has asked anybody anything in any chapter of this volume.** It found that the clerk of about fifty-five in Chapter 0376 is in the same room, with the same woman of about nineteen and the same note on the same shelf, as the same clerk in Chapter 0370 six chapters earlier, and that the two chapters' exchanges are now materially different, and the overlap is entered as a structural debt rather than left as a fact. It found that the chapter ending with the count of askings printed in words, where the batch's own craft instruction said not to print a figure for the count other than none, is **Chapter 0376 and not Chapter 0377, which the first version of this record said and which was carried wrong into three other files, all four of which are now corrected beside the wrong figure**; that is a breach of an instruction and not of a lock, and it is left in the page and entered here, and no person is a party to it and no state of any person changes because a number was printed in a closing line. It named three passages as the best of the ten and they are the same three this record already names: the woman on her own step in 0378, the clerk of fifty-five discovering in 0376 that her own rule does not hold for her, and the second of the four women in 0372 saying there is nobody to take it to. **No person in this batch changed state, and the count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is seven at Chapter 350, 360, 370 and 380.**

# THE CHARACTER STATE AT CHAPTER 390, AFTER VOLUME 08 BATCH 0004

*Appended downward by the run that took `workspace/volume-08/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`. Nothing above this point is rewritten. This is a writing phase's record of what these ten weeks did to the people in them, and it is not a review.*

## Marek Kest, thirty-eight, and what the ten weeks cost him

**He is on the page in three of the ten and the floor `outline/volume-08.md` sets for a movement is met.** In 0382 he is an absence with a named cost in somebody else's legs: a clerk of about fifty-five stands in the doorway at the back of her own room and does not walk four hundred yards of her own passage, and the reason she gives out loud is that if she walks them a woman of twenty-six at that lock end will be told by her boots that somebody at this end has finally gone and looked, and the man the looking would be for is a man of thirty-eight who came the whole of that distance on his own two feet once in a month that is gone and was not entered. In 0385 he is an absence with a named cost a second time, said out loud once to a passage: there is a room at the far end of those flags with a rail in it about four feet off the floor and nothing on it, and a man of thirty-eight has been in that building and has not been in that room, and those two things are not going anywhere together. **In 0390 he is in person in a rented room, and his want is on the page in his own voice, and the stopping is the only one in the movement that is his.**

**What changed in him, and it is the whole of what changed: a clerk of about fifty-five came the whole way to his rented room and told him out loud, once, that the hand which struck his mother's name out of that line is a hand he could name. He did not write it down. She did not say it a second time. Neither of them said what happens next. He did not go and look. He did not thank her and she is not thanked, and the two of them agreed out loud, both of them, that neither was going to, which is the closest thing to an arrangement in this volume and it is not one.** **The distance is not given on the page and is not given here, and the phrase this paragraph carried before it — that she came about four miles — was the writing pass's figure and was removed from Chapter 0390 by the continuation, because closed Chapters 0371, 0376 and 0377 put that room nine miles off and this file was not corrected when the continuation ended, which is the same failure twice and in two files and is entered as item 72.** The name is not printed in the chapter, and neither is his mother's, and the who is Volume 06's and goes on unanswered. **He holds no office, no fund, no list and no leave. He refused the reading and did not exercise the thing he is able to do and is not at a stage, and the mark on the inside of his left wrist is three inches long and does nothing in that room and the narrator says so in a sentence and does not explain it. Nobody asked him one thing. The woman about four miles off at the top of a stair is still not told anything.**

**What he now has that he did not have at Chapter 380 is a second person who knows a thing about him, and the cost of it is not his.** She gave it away for nothing, on her own account, having spent nineteen years being a person nobody asks anything, and she says out loud in that room that a name written down is a name a person can be served with, and that the telling was hers and not a thing he asked her for, and that it has cost her more than the fourpence he paid at a counter for a copy that did not carry the name.

## The clerk of about fifty-five, nineteen years in that room, and the change in her that the volume has been building

**She is the only person in this matter anybody could serve a question on in writing and she is paid nothing for standing ready to have it served, and her own finding is still a note on a shelf under a window with no pane in it, with a day and an hour on it and no full stop and her own name at the foot.** In 0382 she does not go and look. The room at the far end of her own passage has had nothing in it for four days running and she does not know why and she does not walk the four hundred yards to find out, and the cost of the not walking is in her feet and the closing line of the chapter is that they were colder when she came back in than when she went out. She says out loud that she has stood in that doorway twice and turned round both times, and that the not walking is the first thing she has done in that room that was hers.

**And then in 0390 she comes the whole way to a rented room and tells a man of thirty-eight a thing she has had in her eye for about two years, and the page does not say how far it is and neither does this file.** She has not been asked it and nobody asked her to say it and she says so out loud, twice, and she says that the telling was hers and not a thing he asked her for, and that she would like it noticed that she noticed that he did not ask her one question. **The cost of it is the whole of the movement's turn and it is hers: a person who is not asked has not been given a duty, and she has known that since about the seventh month of next year, and she handed herself one anyway, and the chapter is built so that a reader holds the fact that the woman who has spent nineteen years making herself unaskable is the one person in this matter who has now made herself askable, on purpose, for nothing.** She is not thanked. Nobody sent for her. She is not forgiven by anybody because there is nothing to forgive. She did not put the answer to her own question anywhere and it is still a question with no full stop and nobody has asked her a second time.

**And what she has not done is the thing a reader wants: she did not ask him for anything, he did not ask her for anything, and the person she told has not told anybody else, because there is nobody else he would tell and she has not said one word about it to the woman of about nineteen who watches her come out of that room four times in two days.**

## The rest of the people, and what ten weeks did to them

**Rennick Adley, thirty-eight, the book-keeper, who has wanted the rest of a sentence since the third month of the year after.** In 0381 he writes the half of it out again at the top of a column he has ruled himself on a sheet of his own, with nothing printed over any of the three lines, and it comes out the same as it came out before, and he stops where it stops. **A clerk of about twenty-nine at the other end of those two tables says out loud that a column with nothing printed over it is a thing a person can be handed, and that he has been writing at the top of it for two years and has never once said whether the half he has is the whole of what she said to him.** He did not say. She said once that she would not say a second thing. **The change: there are two people in that Company who know what is at the top of that column, and there were one.** He did not walk the four hundred yards down the lane to the shed, he did not thank her, and the second half of the sentence did not arrive.

**Halla Wray, fifty-one, a foreman in a box entered by a class, never asked.** In 0386 a form arrives that would take the two of seventeen at the back of that shed out of the back of it, and the form has a heading that takes a class and a heading that takes a place and **no space anywhere on it for a person of seventeen to put no in.** She got as far as saying out loud that there is no form for a form that has not been found void and the man of about twenty-eight stopped her, as he stopped her a month ago, and said the same shape of reason a second time and it was a different reason: a form nobody has found void is a form that is still there next week. She said nothing and the form is still there. **The man of about fifty-five with the chain put his hand flat on that form and said his four sentences about the weight of a chain, and the man at the front of the bench read the form out loud over his hand in the ordinary way, and nobody noticed anything about it at all.** Neither of the two at the back was spoken to at any hour of the day and the girl was not given the chance to say no. The second half of the sentence she began in a doorway has still not arrived and the half she did give the book-keeper four hundred yards up the lane did not arrive in 0386 either.

**Tamsin Rook, twenty-four, at the fourth counter, with a live and unexercised undertaking.** In 0383 the fourth of the four things in the drawer is on her counter at the fourth hour and is not hers and the woman of thirty-four put it there without knowing whose it was. **Marn Ottery resists the putting back — not by refusing to help, but by telling her that a thing which has been out cannot be put back in, and that about nine people came up those stairs between the fourth hour and now and read the top of a sheet of paper from the rail, and that the two of them are now the reason a piece of paper in a drawer is a piece of paper on a counter.** Tamsin Rook put it back by hand, turned so the date went to the back, and shut the drawer with the flat of her hand and not with a noise. The undertaking is neither exercised nor retired, it is still live, nobody has retired it, and **neither of the two of them said that date out loud.** The box of about four hundred blanks under the far end of that counter came out for a bundle of string and went back and was not opened. Nobody asked her anything and nobody thanked her.

**Nell Kest, twenty, about four hundred and thirty miles up this river, sixteen books, a wage held.** In 0387 a notice arrives with a sum on it and nothing said against what, and on the back of it a printed ruled space with a heading over it that takes the reason, and it is a notice and not a form and the difference between those two things is the whole of the chapter. **She did not fill it in. The second of the four women, who has not been in that room in four days and does not come into it, stopped her in the lane and told her that a reason a person is owed is a reason a person can be held against, and that once it is in there it belongs to whoever printed the heading, and said it once and went down the lane.** The notice went back with the boy who brought it and is in a coat four miles off by the end of the week with the other nineteen days. She did not ask the man what any of it is sitting against, she is not going to, she is not going up this river this season, and that is not never. Nobody paid her, nobody sent for her, nobody thanked her, the sixteenth book was not opened, and the rule was not given to a third woman.

**The man of about thirty-four with the fair hand, the only person anybody could put a question to and the one nobody has.** In 0389 a form comes through the letter slot of the house with four steps, with a ruled space and a heading printed over it in type, and the heading is the kind of word a person picks out of three others in a few minutes while thinking about a column and not about anybody, and the figure in his head is the only thing in this empire that would go in it. **He put it back down on the hall floor and did not fill it in, and it is not returned, and nobody in that lane returned it.** The woman of about thirty-five next door, who has been next door eleven years, knocked on his door for the second time in eleven years and said out loud that a form left in a hall is a form somebody will come back for, and that a man who lets a form sit in his hall for a month has a fact about himself on that form whether he fills it in or not, and that she has watched him not do things for eleven years and has never said a word about any of it. He did not tell her where he had been, she did not ask, she said she is not going to be thanked for the knock and would rather he did not open his mouth about it, and the form is still not returned.** The number is not printed, not confirmed and not denied. Nobody thanked anybody. The four feet of lane and the two doors were not described and the Lowcross figure was not named.

**The man of about sixty-one, second hand since he was forty-two, who cannot read a paragraph and signs about nine hundred of them a year.** In 0388 a young man of about twenty-six who carries the sheets away hands him one that is not the ordinary one, and for about four seconds he has it in his hand with the pen out, and he does not sign it, and it goes down the corridor in the basket and the basket does not come back up. **The young man says out loud that he is paid for a corner and not for a page, and that a man at the foot of a sheet who has the pen out for four seconds is a man who is about to be asked one thing in nineteen years, and that he has carried that basket down that corridor every time and has never once seen an asking happen.** The man of about sixty-one then says out loud, afterwards, that he has never told anybody that he knows which ones are wrong, and that this is the first time in nineteen years he has said it to another person, and that he has said it to a man paid by the corner who will be gone in a year, and that it is not the four minutes. **His own figure is neither confirmed nor denied, he did not walk the nine miles, nobody asked him anything, nobody thanked him, and the door went on the latch.**

**The woman of about twenty-six at the end of four hundred yards of cold flags, with a figure that has gone up.** In 0385 a sheet comes down that passage with a ruled space on it and a heading printed over the space, and the ruled space is the size of a count and not the size of a name, and she is the only person at that end of the passage who has the thing that would go in it. **She did not write it, and the stopping was her own hand and not anybody's refusal, and the sheet went back up the passage in somebody else's hand with the space as clean as it came off the press.** The man in the felt apron told her, and this is the second time in this volume he has resisted her keeping a number: he was not going to tell her not to write it, and he was going to tell her that a number under a heading is a number that belongs to whoever printed the heading, and that she has been the only person at that end who could be made to give one up. She did not write it and did not deny that it exists. **The figure is not printed in any form, gets no heading, and the four hundred yards is not walked.**

**The three people the state layer keeps apart stayed apart, and the check is the point of this entry.** The man of about sixty-one is nine miles off and is in Chapter 0388. The woman of about twenty-six is at the end of that cold passage and is in Chapter 0385, and in Chapter 0382 she is referred to from a room four hundred yards off and is not in it. The woman of twenty is about four hundred and thirty miles up the river and is in Chapter 0387, and she was on a lane outside her own counting room with the second of the four women and with nobody else. **A woman of about forty with a scuttle is a fourth person and is in none of these ten files, which is entered and is not a repair.** The clerk of about fifty-five and the man of about sixty-one are in the same building and may be in the same room, and this batch did not put them in one room, and Chapter 0390 is the first of this volume in which the two of them are in the same building at all.

**And the people who are not in these ten files, entered so a later reader does not read it as an omission:** the girl of seventeen and the reader of seventeen, who are unspoken to, unthanked, unsent for and not going to start; the four who cannot make sense of a paragraph, not one of whom was asked a question about a number; the woman of about forty with a scuttle, who cannot be asked what she heard and is in none of these ten files; the woman of about thirty-five with the day-book's younger woman of twenty-two, who said a thing out loud once in Chapter 0373 and did not say it again in this batch; and the thirty-one who hold the same road and were not on it.

**Nobody in this matter was thanked. Nobody was forgiven. Nobody was sent for. Nothing was resolved. The count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is seven at Chapter 350, 360, 370, 380 and 390, and it is not printed in any of these ten chapters, and the next asking is nobody's yet.**

## The four debts, restated at the end of Batch 0004, and it discharges none of them

**An owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, four volumes on. Owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's own Batch 0003, which is four and not three. The review gate, which opened and produced findings on all seven Batch 0005 phases the commit history records, and has fallen back to the writing agent every time it has been asked, and has never once produced a review that could be certified independent, and a gate that only ever falls back is not a gate that passed either. And the open items that are not plot, with the sentence bound among them.** The number of debts has not moved in eight volumes and the number of phases that have claimed to discharge one is zero, and no person in this record is a party to any of them.

## THE CONTINUATION OF BATCH 0004, WHAT CHANGED ABOUT THESE PEOPLE WHEN ELEVEN SENTENCES WERE RE-VOICED, AND WHAT DID NOT CHANGE AT ALL

*Appended downward by the same run, resumed from its own checkpoint. **Nothing above this point is rewritten.** The writing pass's record of these ten chapters stands above and is not corrected in place; this section says what the repairs did to the people, because three of the eleven repairs touched a person's own defining sentence and a reader who inherits only the writing pass's record would inherit a person who is not the person the page carries.*

**Marek Kest, thirty-eight, the protagonist. Unchanged, and that is what the eleven repairs were for.** He is in two chapters as an absence with a named cost and in one in person, and his want is on the page in his own voice in 0390 and in nobody else's. **The one thing this continuation removed from him was a distance he was never given: the clerk of about fifty-five's building is about nine miles off his rented room, and 0390 no longer says she came four.** He is not thanked, he asks nothing, nobody asks him anything, he does not reach a stage, he does not exercise the thing he is able to do, and the name that ought to stand at the end of that struck line is not printed and the name of the hand that struck it is not printed and his mother's name is not printed.

**The man of about sixty-one, second hand since he was forty-two, who signs about nine hundred sheets a year and cannot read a paragraph. The person this continuation changed most, and it changed him toward the page.** What he says out loud in 0388 is not the first thing he has ever said out loud: it is that he has said one of them at a time, more than once in nineteen years, and has never once said that there was a way of telling them apart. **He wanted the first reading and the page does not support it, because closed Chapter 0368 has him speaking a whole thing in that room more than once and putting a sentence into that same carrier, and a man who has spoken a whole thing has not been silent for nineteen years.** He still does not sign the sheet. He still has about four minutes and still has nobody to give them to. The carrier in 0388 has been doing it two years, which is where 0368 puts him, and the closing of 0388 no longer names a day the chapter is not dated to.

**The man of about fifty-five with the chain, fifty-one, or about fifty-five, at the front bench of the shed. Set back inside a lock he was written out of by four words.** He is not one of the four who cannot make sense of a paragraph and never was, and 0386 now says so by omission rather than by denial: he says there is a thing on the paper that is not a figure and is not a name and that he has never once worked out what it is for, and that the four of them behind him could not tell you either. He still says four sentences, they still take him as long as turning a hand over, and the paper under his hand is still the weight of a folded sheet and a chain he has worn since he was a young man. **He was not asked one question about a number, and he was not asked anything at all, and the form is still on the board and is still not found void by anybody.**

**Tamsin Rook, twenty-four, at the fourth counter. Her own account of the wanting is now her own words.** What she has been unable to arrive at, on a slow afternoon with Marn Ottery, is whether the wanting was put into her or grew there, and a thing a person wants is not a debt anybody can be called on for. The two of them are still two people who have never said the date of the fourth of the first month to each other, the fourth of the four things is still in the drawer, the drawer was shut by the fifth hour, and the undertaking is still live and unretired and unexercised and is still hers and only hers. Nobody asked her anything, she asked nothing, and the box of about four hundred bought blanks under the far end of that counter came out and went back and was not opened.

**Marn Ottery, thirty-four, at the other end of the same counter. Unchanged.** She is the resister in 0383 and she is right about the drawer and wrong about the remedy being available, and she says out loud that the two of them can be the reason together instead of one at a time, which is a worse arrangement and the only one on offer.

**The woman of thirty-five with the day-book, and the woman of twenty-two in the back room. The coming is given to the right person.** Two years ago a woman came nine miles to that step and was not let in, and this woman heard all of it from behind the counter and has written none of it down and has not been asked about it. **The first draft of that sentence had her standing on the step and hearing from behind the counter at once, which was two people in one subject, and it is corrected, and the correction matters to her because the whole of what she is is that she was the one behind the counter.** The day-book came down off the shelf at the end of 0384 and stayed out on the wood all afternoon with the four headings where they have always been and no fifth column under any of them, and she read nothing in it and the girl of twenty-two read nothing in it.

**The man of about thirty-four, paid for a fair hand. Unchanged, and the one thing this continuation did to his chapter was take a chronology out of it.** A form came through a letter slot and was not filled in and was not returned; it was still on the hall floor on the second day, it was gone by the middle of the week after, and nobody in that lane knows who took it or where it went. He did not tell the woman next door where he had been and she did not ask. His number is not printed and was not printed and did not go in the space, and four months from now somebody turns that sheet over at a desk and the space under the heading is blank and the heading does not say whose it was.

**The woman of about thirty-five next door, eleven years in that lane. Unchanged, and the knock is still the second of two in eleven years and the other one was a stove nine years ago.** She said out loud that a form left where a person can find it is a form somebody will come back for, that a man who lets one sit has a fact about himself on it whether he fills it in or not, and that she is not going to be thanked for the knock because a thank you is a debt and she did not come to be owed one. **The one question this continuation took out of her mouth was *What is it then*, which was a question, and it is now *I know what it is and you know I know, and that is the whole of why you have knocked*, and nothing else about her changed and the count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is still seven.**

**The woman of about twenty-six at the end of four hundred yards of cold flags. Unchanged, and her count is still hers.** A sheet came down to her with a ruled space about an inch and a half deep and a heading printed over it that anybody could use, and she read the heading twice and handed it back without a mark on it, and it went back up the passage at the fourth hour with the space as clean as it came off the press. The man in the felt apron said out loud that a number under a heading is a number that belongs to whoever printed the heading, and that she has been the only person at that end who could be made to give one up. **The passage is now referred to in her chapter rather than described for the third time in the volume, and the lock and the rail four hundred yards off are still the two things in it.** Her figure is not printed and did not go in the space and the man in the felt apron did not carry it up the flags.

**Nell Kest, twenty, about four hundred and thirty miles up this river. Unchanged.** A notice with a sum on it came to that bench and on the back of it there was a printed ruled space for the reason, and the reason was not written in and the notice went back down the lane in a boy's hand and into the coat four miles off with the other nineteen days. The second of the four women said out loud that a reason a person is owed is a reason a person can be held against, and she has not been in that room in four days and did not come in that week. She is not paid, she did not ask what it is held against, she is not going up this river this season, and that is not never.

**Halla Wray, fifty-one, a foreman inside a box entered by a class, and not a holder of anything anybody can be served with. Unchanged.** The form came round the front of the shed and went on the board and the man of about twenty-eight stopped her from saying out loud what it would do to the two at the back, and this time he gave a different reason, and the different reason was that a form nobody has found void is a form that is still there next week. She said nothing. The form is still on the board and it will be on that board again on the second of the two days next week, and the girl of seventeen was not given the chance to say no.

**Rennick Adley, thirty-eight, the book-keeper, who wants the rest of one sentence and has not asked. Unchanged.** He wrote the half at the top of a column with nothing printed over it and stopped where it stops, and a clerk of about twenty-nine at the other end of the joined tables said out loud that a column with nothing printed over it is a thing a person can be handed, and that a not-saying kept up for two years is a thing two people know about. **From this week there are two people in that Company who know whose words are at the top of that third column, and one of them did not know that this morning, and the second sentence did not arrive and was not going to arrive.**

**The clerk of about fifty-five, nineteen years in one room, whose own question is still a question with no full stop on it. The one person this continuation gave something to.** She did not walk ten yards of her own passage this week although the room at the far end of it has had nothing in it for four days, and the not walking is in her legs and in the cold at the one corner of the window that has not moved in a month. **And at the end of this batch she came the whole way to a rented room and said one thing out loud that she has never said in her own room, and it was the hand that struck a name out of a line, and she did not say it twice and she did not write it down, and the clerk of about fifty-five's own question on her shelf is still a question with a day and an hour on it and no full stop, and her own name is still the only name at the foot of anything in that building.**

**The people who are not in these ten files, and this continuation confirms the omission rather than closing it:** the girl of seventeen and the reader of seventeen, unspoken to, unthanked, unsent for and not going to start; the four who cannot make sense of a paragraph, not one of whom was asked a question about a number; the woman of about forty with a scuttle, who cannot be asked what she heard and is in none of these ten files and is not the woman of about twenty-six at the end of the cold passage; and the thirty-one who hold the same road and were not on it. **No two of the three people this state layer keeps apart are in one room in any of these ten files, and the woman of twenty is about four hundred and thirty miles up this river in a furnace town and is in the same batch as the other two in a file and not in a room.**

**Nobody in this matter was thanked. Nobody was forgiven. Nobody was sent for. Nothing was resolved. The count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is seven at Chapter 350, 360, 370, 380 and 390, and it is not printed in any of these ten chapters, and the next asking is nobody's yet.**

## VOLUME 08, BATCH 0005, CHAPTERS 0391 TO 0400 — THE PEOPLE AT FOUR HUNDRED CHAPTERS, WRITTEN BY THE WRITING PHASE THAT WROTE THEM

**This section was written by the run that wrote the ten chapters, after the last of its edits to the page. It is a state record and not a review, and it discharges none of the four debts. Nothing above this point is rewritten.**

**Marek Kest, thirty-eight, the protagonist.** Audits contracts and inheritances for an empire that does not pay him for it. No office, no money, no roll, no leave, nothing whatever standing behind him. **He refuses the reading, does not exercise the thing he is able to do, and is not at a stage.** He wants the erased name of his mother and the disputed family claim and has neither. In 0399 a woman who keeps the ground floor of the house he rents told him once that a person who knows a thing and does not write it down has to be the person who remembers it, and that he is one of two people in this empire keeping a thing in that condition, and that the other one is her. **She is not the clerk of about fifty-five who came the whole of that distance in 0390, and the chapter now says so in the paragraph that introduces her, and she does not know what his thing is and did not put it where it is.** Nine weeks stand between the two chapters and not one week. **He did not write it down and did not go and look, and he is not thanked, and nobody asked him anything, and he asked nobody anything.** The name of the hand that struck the line is in his head and on no paper, and the name itself is printed in none of these ten chapters. He is on the page in three of them: 0392 and 0398 as an absence with a cost named in somebody else's mouth, and 0399 in person.

**The woman of about thirty-five next door, thirty-five years old and eleven years in that house.** On the page in her own right for the first time in this volume in 0397. Keeps a day-book in her own hand with a printed heading over every column and no fifth column ruled in it in nine years. Knocked twice on that door in eleven years and said out loud that she would not knock a third time, and the man on the other side of the four feet has told her that the not knocking is now hers to answer for. **She has never once been inside that hall and did not go up the four steps at the end of 0397.** She is not thanked and she has said she does not want to be. **She is not the woman of about twenty-six at the end of the cold passage and is not the woman of about forty with a scuttle, and no chapter of this batch puts any two of the three in one room, and the scuttle woman is in none of these ten files, which is the third batch running and is entered and is not a repair.**

**Marn Ottery, thirty-four, at one end of a counter at the top of four flights, eleven years, not a lawyer.** In 0391 she moved a box of about four hundred bought and unfilled blanks four inches and then an inch back, and a young man from the shipping floor told her out loud that a clerk who moves a box is a clerk who has made a statement about it, and she said the statement was hers and that nobody had asked her for a decision. **In 0400 she told the woman at the other end of the same counter that a person who has been ready for two years has made herself the only thing in that room that can be said something to, and that readiness is not a form and cannot be entered on anything, and that the day somebody at the top of those four flights finds out is the day she gets used, and that she was saying it as a woman telling a colleague and not as somebody with any standing to do one thing about it.** She has said the shape of a thing out loud in that room twice and been asked one thing back twice, said a third thing to a colleague and been asked nothing, and has said once that she will not say a second thing. She is not thanked. **Her want, which the state layer has never printed plainly and this section prints for the first time: that the two women at that counter go on being the only two who know what is in that drawer, and that the arrangement that makes that true keeps standing.**

**Tamsin Rook, twenty-four, at the other end of that counter, two years, not a lawyer.** Nobody has ever put a question to her in that time. **Her undertaking of the fourth of the first month is live, unretired and unexercised, and the seventeen weeks since the fourth of the four things came out onto that counter and went back in unopened have not touched it.** In 0400 she was told out loud that she is ready, and she did not exercise it, did not retire it and did not say the date out loud, and she did one smaller thing: at a quarter past the fourth hour she put her hand flat on the boards, and then she took her apron off the hook and hung it up again, because she had come in twenty minutes early for two years and there had been no need to. **The last line of the volume is that nothing is asked of anybody and the arrangement is still standing when she gets to the bottom of the steps.**

**The clerk of about fifty-five, nineteen years in one room off the old river road.** Writes the minutes, advises nobody, asks nothing. Her own finding is on a shelf under a window with no pane in it, a question with no full stop and her own name at the foot. **In 0392, the fourth time she is on the page in this volume and the first time she is in it with nothing to withhold, she said one true thing out loud in that room that is not about the sheets — a fact about the wind having gone round to the other side of the building — and the woman of about nineteen wrote none of it down and has said nothing about it since.** The woman of about nineteen told her that a person who tells is a person who has to go on telling, and that the not being asked has cost her the only thing she had, which was not being asked, and she did not answer it. **She is the only person in this matter anybody could serve a question on in writing, she made herself askable on purpose in 0390, and nothing has happened to her that anybody can be told about in these ten weeks.**

**The man of about thirty-four with the fair hand.** The only person in this matter anybody could put a question to, and no one has. A number in his head that has never been written and never handed on, and a form with a printed heading over a ruled space came through a letter slot in 0389 and was not returned. **In 0397 he told the woman next door that he is not going to forget that she knocked, and that there is no form for the second knock of the two, and that she has made herself responsible for the not knocking, and that he has decided to put it on her because he has nobody he could hand it to.** He filled in nothing, he told her nothing about where he has been, and the number is not printed, not confirmed and not denied. **He is not Rennick Adley and he is not Marek Kest, both of whom are thirty-eight, and no chapter of this batch names any of the three by his age.**

**Rennick Adley, thirty-eight, the Company's book-keeper, four hundred yards up the lane off the Slade.** Wants the rest of a sentence and has not asked and is not going to. **In 0398, the second of his two chapters in this volume, he wrote the same four lines out for the eleventh time and they came out the same and he stopped where they stop.** The clerk of about twenty-nine did not say a second thing. She told him out loud that the woman at the Slade end is still in a box that a class put her in and that a box entered by a class does not come out of itself, and that there is nobody he could tell and no form for telling and no standing for telling, and that the not telling is not a decision he made this week. **She also said the one thing in these ten chapters about Marek Kest: that he is the only person anybody knows of who could walk down that lane without it being a record of it, and that he is not in that building.** He did not walk the four hundred yards and nobody thanked anybody.

**Halla Wray, fifty-one, a foreman at the Slade end, entered there by a class, which is a heading.** **In 0395 a form that would take the two of seventeen at the back out of the back of that shed had been on the board since the tenth month, and she got as far as saying out loud that it had been there a month, and the man of about twenty-eight stopped her a third time and gave a third reason: there is no form for saying a month, and a foreman who says a month out loud in a shed with about nine men in it has told nine men about two people at the back of it.** She said nothing. The two of seventeen are still on that bench, the form is still on that board and will be on it again on the second of the two days next week, the girl was not given the chance to say no, the man of about fifty-five with the chain said his four sentences and nobody answered him and he never wanted an answer, the lamp was not lit until the seventh hour, and the chalk mark is still a mark. The second half of the sentence she began in a doorway has still not arrived.

**The man of about sixty-one, the second hand since he was forty-two.** Signs at the foot of about nine hundred sheets a year and cannot read a paragraph. About nine of them a year are not what the man above him wrote, he signed every one, and he is the only person alive who knows which. **In 0396 a young man of about twenty-six who is paid by a corner and not for a page told him out loud that a man who has told one person one true thing in nineteen years has a second one in him, and that the second one will come out in about four months, and that the corridor with the hook in the wall is the only place it will happen, and that the corner is the only part of it he is responsible for. He answered it once, to the pen pot: a man does not get the pen out twice in a week for a thing he did not do the first time.** The second true thing did not arrive this week and his own figure is neither confirmed nor denied and no coming was dated or counted by anybody who can be held to it, and the nine miles is not walked.

**The woman of about twenty-six at the end of about four hundred yards of cold flags.** Four years at that end, never one of the three clerks, never sent for, thanked or handed a sheet. Keeps a figure in her head that is on no paper and is going to nobody. **In 0393 nothing with a heading on it came down that passage in a week, and the man in the felt apron told her out loud that the arrangement is the arrangement and that the only thing that has happened in four years is that a sheet came down once and went back up, and that a person waiting for the next one has made the first one into something that happened, and that she is the only person at that end who could turn that into something that costs her.** She said out loud, once, to the passage, that she is not going to be a person who waits for the next one, and was not at the lock for three of his four journeys. The figure is not printed in any form and does not get a heading and has not gone down. The room at the far end of those flags is not entered and the four hundred yards is not walked.

**Nell Kest, twenty, about four hundred and thirty miles up this river.** Sixteen volumes on that bench, fifteen borrowed out of other rooms, the one with her own name in capitals at the head of it. Nineteen days of a crew's money in a coat four miles off in a room she has not been inside since she was seventeen, and a notice with a printed ruled space on the back of it in the same pocket with the space clean. **In 0394 the coat went four miles and came back four miles and the money is the same, and the second of the four women came back into that room on the first of the two days and told her out loud that nobody picked the rule up, that the not being picked up is the most expensive thing anybody has done to her, and that a rule said once in the open lives in the person who heard it and not in the person who said it, and that the second of the two of them has to go on carrying the furniture.** She is not thanked, is not forgiven, asks for nothing, and is not going up this river this season, and a season is not a life. Nobody paid her, nobody sent for her, the ruled space was not filled in, the rule was not given to a third woman, and the one with her name on the head of it was not opened.

**The three the state layer keeps apart, kept apart again.** A man of about sixty-one is nine miles off a building off the old river road and is neither of the two women. The woman of about twenty-six at the end of that cold passage is a third person. The woman of twenty is about four hundred and thirty miles the other way in a furnace town. **No chapter of this batch puts any two of the three in one room and no chapter states or denies a distance between any two of them, and the clerk of about fifty-five and the man of about sixty-one are in the same building and are not in the same room in any of these ten chapters.**

**New to the ledger with this batch, and none of them a named figure.** A young man from the shipping floor who came up to leave a sheet in 0391 and said out loud that he was a party to a statement about a box. **The woman who keeps the ground floor of the house Marek Kest rents, who has been at the bottom of those stairs about twenty years and has never been above the second one, and who is now the second person in this matter known to be keeping a thing with no form on it. She is a third person and the state layer keeps three apart: her, and the clerk of about fifty-five who keeps minutes about four hundred yards off the old river road and has been in that room nineteen years, and Marek Kest himself. The first draft of Chapter 0399 had her claiming to be the woman who put his thing where it is and saying she had not slept properly since she did it, which merged the first two; the chapter now says in its own fourth paragraph that she is not the woman who came nine weeks ago and has never been inside that building, and the claim in her mouth is now a claim about her own carrying and not about his.** The count of consecutive batches that have added no named figure stands at twenty-five and this makes it **twenty-six**, and a standard is not an oversight.

---

## VOLUME 08 CLOSED AT CHAPTER 0400 — THE PEOPLE AT THE END OF FIFTY WEEKS, WRITTEN BY THE CLOSE AND NOT BY A BATCH

**This section was written by the close phase that took `workspace/volume-08/volume-close/PROMPT.md`. It is a state record and not a review, it discharged none of the four debts, and it opened no chapter. Nothing above this point is rewritten.** One entry per principal, at Chapter 0400, in the state each of them is actually in on the page and not in the state a summary would like them in. Where a figure appears it was measured per file by this close and the method is named in `state/continuity.md`.

**Marek Kest, thirty-eight, the protagonist.** He wants the erased name of his mother and the disputed family claim and has neither, and **the name of the hand that struck the line out of the leaf in his coat is in his head and on no paper and is printed in no chapter of this volume.** He holds nothing: no office, no fund, no list, no leave, no stage. He refuses the reading, does not exercise the thing he is able to do, and his reason for not reaching a stage is in about nine words on a page and is not paid a second time. He has a mark about three inches long on the inside of his left wrist that reads nothing and decides nothing. **He is on the page in eleven of the volume's fifty chapters and three a movement in the last three of the five, which is the floor, and in one a movement in the first two, which is a shortfall this close names and does not repair.** In person he is in 0371, 0379, 0390 and 0399; as an absence with a cost named in somebody else's mouth he is in 0377, 0382, 0385, 0392 and 0398. Nobody asked him anything, he asked nobody anything, and he is not thanked. **A woman who keeps the ground floor of the house he rents told him once, in Chapter 0399, that he is one of two people in this empire keeping a thing in that condition and that the other one is her; he did not write it down, did not go and look, and is not thanked for it. She is not the clerk of about fifty-five and the page now says so where she is introduced.**

**The protagonist's mother, dead on the roll and not dead on the page.** Gone twelve years in the Ashfall Archive fire. **Her name appears nowhere in this file, and nowhere in Volume 08's fifty chapters, and this close neither prints it nor repeats it from the closed page, and neither do they print the name that ought to stand at the end of the struck line or the name of the hand that struck it. The who is Volume 06's and goes on unanswered. Unanswered is not the same as closed.** Her name comes back in Volume 12 and she is not turned into an infallible martyr when it does.

**Bren Kest, alive, Marek's father, a locksmith in Greyfen.** He does not understand dragon law and refuses to pretend that he does. He is not in Volume 08's fifty files and nothing in them moved him.

**Nell Kest, twenty, about four hundred and thirty miles up this river, in a counting room at the back of a dye end.** Sixteen volumes on that bench, fifteen borrowed out of other rooms, the one with her own name in capitals at the head of it, unopened. **Nineteen days of a crew's money is in a coat four miles off, in a room she has not stood in since she was seventeen, and a notice with a printed ruled space on the back of it is in the same pocket with the space clean.** The coat went four miles in Chapter 0394 and came back four miles and the money is the same. A rule said once in a lane lives in her and not in the woman who said it. Nobody paid her, nobody sent for her, nobody thanked her, nobody forgave her, the rule was not given to a third woman, and she is not going up this river this season. **She is not going to ask what it is held against, and that decision is hers and is not a decision this close has any business recording as an improvement.**

**Tamsin Rook, twenty-four, at one end of a counter at the top of four flights, two years, not a lawyer.** **Nobody has ever put a question to her in that time, and that is the whole of her position in this matter.** Her undertaking of the fourth of the first month is live, has never been retired and has never been exercised, and its date has not been said out loud in two years and is not said out loud by this close either. In the last chapter of the volume a colleague told her out loud that a person who has been ready for two years has made herself the only thing in that room that can be said something to, and that readiness is not a form and cannot be entered on anything, and that the day somebody at the top of those four flights finds out is the day she gets used. **She did one smaller thing, at a quarter past the fourth hour: she took her apron off the hook and hung it up again, because she had come in twenty minutes early for two years and there had been no need to.** There is no form for a person being ready, and wanting something nobody has agreed to give her does not make her a person anybody can be called on, and that is true and is not the same as being asked for. **She is not asked. The last line of the volume is that nothing is asked of anybody.**

**Marn Ottery, thirty-four, at the other end of that counter, eleven years, not a lawyer.** About four hundred bought and unfilled blanks under the far end of those boards, unopened in six years, standing three inches further forward than it stood ten weeks ago. **In Chapter 0391 she moved that box four inches and then an inch back, and a young man from the shipping floor told her out loud that a clerk who moves a box is a clerk who has made a statement about it, and she said the statement was hers and that nobody had asked her for a decision. In Chapter 0400 she told Tamsin Rook the plainest thing anybody says to her in four chapters, and she entered that she was saying it as a woman telling a colleague and not as somebody with any standing to do one thing about it, and that she wanted both halves heard together.** She is the only other person in that building who knows what is in that drawer, and the arrangement that makes that true is what she wants, and it is not on the page as a want and is not to be printed as one. She is not thanked, and in the last chapter she says in terms that she is not going to be thanked for it.

**Halla Wray, fifty-one, a foreman at the Slade end, entered there by a class, which is a heading.** A form that would take the two of seventeen at the back of that shed out of the back of it has been on the board since the tenth month, and no one in that lane has the standing to find anything void. **She got as far as saying out loud in Chapter 0395 that it had been there a month, and the man of about twenty-eight stopped her a third time and gave a third reason, and the third was the plainest: there is no form for saying a month, and a foreman who says a month out loud in a shed with about nine men in it has told nine men about two people at the back of it.** She said nothing. The two of seventeen are still on that bench, the girl was not given the chance to say no, the reader of seventeen is unspoken to, unthanked and unsent for, the lamp was not lit until the seventh hour, and the second half of a sentence she began in a doorway has still not arrived. **The man of about fifty-five with the chain is not one of the four who cannot make sense of a paragraph and never is.**

**Rennick Adley, thirty-eight, the Company's book-keeper, four hundred yards up the lane off the Slade.** He wants the rest of a sentence a woman of fifty-one began in a doorway and has not asked and is not going to. **In Chapter 0398 he wrote the same four lines out for the eleventh time and they came out the same and he stopped where they stop.** The clerk of about twenty-nine did not say a second thing, having said once that she would not, and she told him out loud that a box entered by a class does not come out of itself and that there is nobody he could tell and no form for telling and no standing for telling. **She also said the one thing in the closing movement about Marek Kest: that he is the only person anybody knows of who could walk down that lane without it being a record of it, and that he is not in that building.** He did not walk the four hundred yards and nobody thanked anybody. He is not the man of about thirty-four with the fair hand and no chapter of this volume names two men of thirty-eight by their ages in one room.

**The man of about thirty-four with the fair hand.** The only person in this matter anybody could put a question to and the one nobody has. Copies other men's figures until two sets agree, four days a season, on somebody's list. **A number lives in his head that has never been written down and never passed on, and a form with a printed heading over a ruled space came through his letter slot in Chapter 0389 and was not returned and nobody in that lane knows who lifted it.** In Chapter 0397 he told the woman next door that he is not going to forget that she knocked, that there is no form for the second knock of the two, that she has made herself responsible for the not knocking, and that he has put it on her because he has nobody he could hand it to and nothing he could fill in. He filled in nothing and told her nothing about where he has been, and the number is not printed, not confirmed and not denied.

**The man of about sixty-one, the second hand since he was forty-two.** Signs at the foot of about nine hundred sheets a year and cannot read a paragraph. **About nine of them a year are not what the person above him wrote, he signed every one, and he is the only person alive who knows which and cannot write one down.** A young man of about twenty-six who is paid by the corner and not for a page told him out loud in Chapter 0396 that a man who has told one person one true thing in nineteen years has a second one in him, that it will come out in about four months, and that the corridor with the hook in the wall is the only place it will happen. **He answered once, to the pen pot: a man does not get the pen out twice in a week for a thing he did not do the first time.** The second true thing did not arrive, his own figure is neither confirmed nor denied, and the nine miles is not walked. He is neither of the two women in their boxes and is not one of the four who cannot make sense of a paragraph.

**The clerk of about fifty-five, nineteen years in one room off the old river road.** Writes the minutes, advises nobody, asks nothing. Her own finding is on a shelf under a window with no pane in it: a question with no full stop and her own name at the foot, and the only thing on that shelf with a name at the foot. **She is the only person in this matter anybody could serve a question on in writing, and she made herself askable on purpose in Chapter 0390.** In Chapter 0392, the first time she is on the page in this volume with nothing to withhold, she said one true thing out loud in that room that is not about the sheets — a fact about the wind having gone round to the other side of the building — and the woman of about nineteen wrote none of it down and has said nothing about it since. The woman of about nineteen told her that a person who tells is a person who has to go on telling, and that the not being asked has cost her the only thing she had, which was not being asked. She did not answer it. **She did not walk the four hundred yards of her own passage in 0392, and a man of thirty-eight who came the whole of that distance once in 0390 has not been in that building since. Her own question stays a question with no full stop and nobody has asked her a second time.**

**The woman of about twenty-six at the end of about four hundred yards of cold flags.** Four years at that end, never one of the three clerks, never sent for, thanked or handed a sheet. Keeps a figure in her head that is on no paper, is not printed in any form, does not get a heading, and has gone up. **In Chapter 0393 nothing with a heading on it came down that passage in a week, and the man in the felt apron told her out loud that the arrangement is the arrangement, that the only thing that has happened in four years is that a sheet came down once and went back up, and that she is the only person at that end who could turn that into something that costs her.** She said out loud, once, to the passage, that she is not going to be a person who waits for the next one. The figure has not gone down, the room at the far end is not entered, and the four hundred yards is not walked by anybody in the closing movement.

**The woman of about thirty-five next door, thirty-five years old, eleven years in that house.** Knocked twice on that door in eleven years and said out loud in a lane that she would not knock a third time, and the man on the other side of the four feet has told her that the not knocking is now hers to answer for. Keeps a day-book in her own hand with a printed heading over every column and no fifth column ruled in it in nine years. **She has never once been inside that hall, and she is not thanked and has said she does not want to be, and there is no form for the second knock of the two.** She is not the woman of about twenty-six at the end of the cold passage and not the woman of about forty with a scuttle.

**The woman who keeps the ground floor of a rented house, about twenty years at the bottom of those stairs, never once above the second one, takes a rent on one day in the week and has never had his name and has never given him hers.** **She is the second person in this matter known to be keeping a thing with no form on it, and she is a third person and not the clerk of about fifty-five: the clerk keeps minutes about four hundred yards off the old river road and has been in that room nineteen years, and this one has never been inside that building and does not know what was said in it.** The first draft of Chapter 0399 had her claiming to be the woman who put his thing where it is and saying she had not slept properly since she did it, which merged two people this state layer had kept apart for eight volumes; the page is repaired and the claim in her mouth is now a claim about her own carrying — she knows it did not get heavier because she has had one of her own going about the same length of time — and the repair is item 79.

**The three the state layer keeps apart, kept apart at the close, and the fourth separation this volume added.** A man of about sixty-one is nine miles off a building off the old river road and is neither of the two women in their boxes; a woman of about twenty-six is at the end of a cold passage nine miles off the lane off the Slade and is a third person; a woman of twenty is about four hundred and thirty miles up this river in a furnace town. **And the woman who keeps a rented house's ground floor is not the clerk of about fifty-five. No two of the three are in one room in any of Volume 08's fifty files, no chapter states or denies a distance between any two of them, and the clerk and the man of about sixty-one are in the same building and are not in the same room in any of the last ten chapters, which is a fourth thing and not a merge.**

**People named in Volume 08 and not moved.** A woman of about forty with a scuttle is in none of the fifty files, which is entered and is not a repair. A woman of about thirty-five with a day-book is on the page once in her own right. A woman of about twenty-two in her second season was in one chapter and is in none of the last ten. A woman of about nineteen who keeps minutes under the clerk of about fifty-five and a clerk of about twenty-nine at the other end of two joined tables are not the clerk of about fifty-five and are not each other. A man of about twenty-eight who stopped a foreman three times, a young man from the shipping floor, a man in a felt apron, a man of about thirty-one on a doorstep nine miles off, and a man at a gate are all named by age and none of them is a device. **The count of consecutive batches that have added no named figure is twenty-six, and a standard is not an oversight.**

### What this close did not do, entered so a later reader does not read this section as a change in the people

**It did not resolve anything and it did not move anybody.** Nobody is thanked, nobody is forgiven, nobody is sent for, nothing is resolved, and the arrangement is still standing. It did not exercise or retire the undertaking, fill in a ruled space, open a box, empty a coat, rule a fifth column, find a form void, or give the figure at the end of the cold passage a heading. It did not print the who's name, the name at the end of a struck line, the name of the hand that struck it, the number in a man's head, the Lowcross figure, or the count of things asked, except once in each of this file's two closing sections where the brief requires it. **It did not merge any two of the people this state layer keeps apart, and it did not open a chapter of Volume 08 or of any closed volume to change a word.**

## THE REVIEW REPAIR OF THE VOLUME 08 CLOSE — WHAT CHANGED ABOUT THE PEOPLE, WHICH IS ONE COUNT, AND NOTHING ELSE

*Appended downward by the review repair, after the close and after every edit the close made. **Nothing above this point is rewritten and no status in any record above is changed.** The full reading is `reviews/volume-08-volume-close.md` and it is where a later phase should look first; this section is here because the roll is the file a Volume 09 writer opens to find out who is on the page, and one figure in it was wrong.*

**Marek Kest is in person in six of Volume 08's fifty chapters, not four.** 0355 in Movement One, 0365 in Movement Two, 0371 and 0379 in Movement Three, 0390 in Movement Four, 0399 in Movement Five. He is in person in every movement of the volume, once in each except Movement Three, which has two. The other five of the eleven chapters the floor counts are absences with a cost named in somebody else's mouth: 0377, 0382, 0385, 0392 and 0398. The close printed four in person and, in a second file, one movement in person against four chapters in another person's mouth, and **both were wrong and both are corrected, with the wrong figure named at each site and not deleted.** The finding the floor arrived with is unchanged and is the reason the correction matters rather than the count: a floor stated as a number can be met by a sentence in somebody else's mouth, and six rooms is not six scenes.

**Nothing else about him moved.** He holds nothing: no office, no fund, no list, no leave, no stage, no certification at any point in eight volumes. He refuses the reading and does not exercise the thing he is able to do, and his reason for not reaching a stage is in about nine words on the page and is not paid a second time. His want — the erased name of his mother and the disputed family claim — is unresolved, and the name of the hand that struck the line out of the leaf in his coat is in his head and on no paper and is printed in no chapter of Volume 08, and unanswered is not the same as closed. **Nobody asked him one thing, nobody thanked him, nobody forgave him and nobody sent for him, and the count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is seven and did not move.**

**And the four debts are restated here for the ninth time, in the form this repair corrected, and this repair discharges none of them.** An owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, four volumes on. Owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's own Batch 0003, which is four and not three. The review gate, which opened and produced findings on all seven Batch 0005 phases the commit history records, and has fallen back to the writing agent every time it has been asked, and has never once produced a review that could be certified independent, and a gate that only ever falls back is not a gate that passed either. And the open items that are not plot, with the sentence bound among them, which are now eighty-nine against a live ledger of sixty-three entries, because twenty-six numbers are in `state/archive/` or were never used, and item 50 is in no ledger in this project at all. **The number of debts has not moved in eight volumes and the number of phases that have claimed to discharge one is zero, and a close that wrote itself a clean bill of independence would have written the thing this project has been warning about for eight volumes.**

# VOLUME 08, BATCH 0002, CHAPTERS 0361 TO 0370 — WHAT AN ORDINARY WEEK COSTS THE PEOPLE WHO ARE NOT ASKED, AND WHAT THE TWENTY-WORD SWEEP FOUND IN IT

The card file is `outline/batches/volume-08-batch-0002.md` and field nine of all ten cards was written from the files after the files were written. The quotation form this batch declares is the strict form, and this record does not report the strict-form test clean on the other one. It is a writing record. It is not a review, it is not a self-audit, it is not a second reading, it is not a close, and it discharges none of the four debts and closes no item.**

## The craft record, first, and ahead of every measurement, and it is four lines long

**Ten of ten chapters carry an exchange in which another person resists what the point of view wants: 0361 a clerk will not say a thing a third time and tells a man standing at her rail that four people are not coming up that stair this week and it is not the weather; 0362 a clerk of thirty refuses to be the one who writes down what a woman was in a room to hear and sends her to the other day of the week; 0363 a lodger of twenty-two tells a woman with a day-book that not writing a thing is the only thing protecting the four of them; 0364 a man of twenty-eight tells a man with a chain that the thing he can do will not tell him what is on a slate, and then the man says the shape of a question out loud to nine men and nothing happens; 0365 a man of thirty-eight tells a clerk that a box carried up four steps is a thing that can be entered and the entering is a list with her name at the foot of it; 0366 a woman refuses to have a rule said to her twice and is refused, and it is said a second time and goes nowhere again; 0367 a man of twenty-eight tells a foreman that her own sentence is true and that a shut gate is doing the work of nine men for nothing; 0368 a young man of twenty-six tells a second hand that he is paid for a corner and not for a page, after being told out loud that a sheet is not what the man above wrote; 0369 a wife at the foot of four steps says out loud that not asking is not the same as not minding and that he has been waiting two years for a thing that is not going to happen; 0370 a woman of nineteen at the far end of a room says a question with no full stop on it is a note and a note can be thrown out, and is told she is right, and does not throw it out.**

**Marek Kest is on the page in one chapter of the ten, 0365, on the stair, carrying a box he was not asked to carry, saying four or five sentences, thanked by nobody and asked nothing back. The volume outline's floor is three chapters a movement and this batch meets it at one, and that is a shortfall and it is named here rather than dressed. The reason is on the page and it is not an accident of the writing: the man of thirty-four with the fair hand and Marek Kest are two different men in two different rooms, and the counter and the stair in 0365 is a room about four miles off from a rented room two streets off a street that goes down to the river road, and putting the protagonist on that stair for a second week would have been a plan rather than a person. The next movement's cards are the place this is made up, and the shortfall is carried into them as a debt and not forgiven.**

**`about` in the ten files, counted whole-word over the prose with the chapter titles excluded, method named because the two readings differ: 9 / 10 / 13 / 16 / 7 / 6 / 13 / 12 / 17 / 12, a batch of 115 in 10,699 words, which is one in ninety-three words. **The ten closings, each written for its chapter and for no other, and no two of them sharing a sentence shape: 0361 the stair has no door at the foot of it and there is nowhere at the bottom of it for a man to stand that is not in the way of somebody going up; 0362 the lamp stayed lit behind her, and it is the only thing on that bank that has ever been on purpose and gone on without her; 0363 the four rooms went on being let by the week, and there is a heading over that column too, and she has never once had to explain it to anybody in this building who has not already known what it is; 0364 the slate is on that bench face up, with a question on it and no name under it, and nine men heard the shape of a question go out of a room at about the sixth hour and it went nowhere, which is the whole of what a bay is; 0365 the box is on the floor of the received room with the lid where it was, and the count is the same as it was when he came up that stair and is not going to be a different number on the way down; 0366 not one person has ever come up those stairs for that book, and nobody has sent for it, and nobody ever has, and she is not going to stand ready for somebody who does not come; 0367 the whole of what the open gate cost that week was four minutes in which nobody in that bay said anything at all, and nobody has put a name at the foot of that either; 0368 the pane is still out of that window and nobody has put it back, and there was a sheet in front of him that would have taken four minutes and there was nobody in the building to give it to; 0369 that is the arrangement in the next house worked in the other direction, and both of them would be inside four feet of the other one inside a minute if either said one word out loud, and neither of them did; 0370 the young woman at the far end of that room has not thrown it out, and neither clerk in it is going to say out loud that she has not thrown it out.**

## What the twenty-word paragraph sweep found in this batch's own ten files, and what was done about it

**The prompt's first instruction was mechanical and it came before a card, and it caught this batch three times. The sweep is a twenty-word window over whole paragraphs run between a new file and the closed page, printing the text and not only the count. The first run of it on these ten files returned 41 maximal shared runs of twenty words or more. Every one of them was real lifted material and every one of it was this batch revoicing a paragraph of Volume 08's own first batch or a closed chapter: a 43-word opening for the counter at the top of four flights out of closed Chapter 0311, a 48-word opening for the cold passage out of closed Chapter 0346, a 30-word sentence about a shelf and a sheet out of the same chapter, a 53-word paragraph about a wage held and a pair of parties out of Chapter 0352, a 48-word paragraph about a hand that does not vary out of the same file, a 41-word run that joined this batch's date line to Chapter 0352's opening, a 33-word and a 32-word opening for Halla Wray out of Chapter 0356, a 31-word run about the men at the front of that bay out of the same chapter, six runs in Chapter 0368 out of Chapters 0357 and 0360 including a 22-word lift of a man's opening and a 22-word lift of the sheet that does not come back up, three runs in Chapter 0369 out of Chapters 0351, 0354 and 0345, and eight runs in Chapter 0370 out of Chapter 0360 including 47, 37, 35 and 32 words.**

**All forty-one were rewritten, in the same rooms, with the same facts, the same locks and the same card beats, and the sentences were written new. A second run returned 22, of which ten were the required dated week and twelve were accidental; those twelve were rewritten. A third run returned 11 and every one of the 11 is a required verbatim recurrence: ten instances of the dated week, one to a file, and the required closing count recurrence in Chapter 0370. The same sweep on Batch 0001 before its repair returned 104 and after it 23. The standing figure for this project is therefore 11 against 104, and the finding is that the check is worth more than a chapter and not worth what a chapter costs to get right the first time.**

**The twelve-word duplicate whole-paragraph test, run on whole paragraphs and read as maximal runs rather than as overlapping windows, returned 119 distinct maximal shared runs of twelve words or more against the closed page. Of the 119, 22 were accidental and were rewritten; the remaining 97 are the required dated week, the required closing count recurrence, the inherited phrase about a person who is not asked, the canon dates of a wage being held and of a class being entered, and the fragmentary tails of those same formulae. The intra-batch twelve-word test returned 52, of which 45 are the dated week and the count recurrence and 7 are the dated week seen at fourteen words; the intra-batch thirty-four-word bound, leave-one-out, returns 0 in all ten files. The blind spot beside all four figures: a maximal-run reading hides a shorter lift that sits inside a longer one, and a count cannot see a room that has been described twice in different words, which is the failure the volume's own second craft rule exists to catch and which no window in this project can see.**
